On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 15 v1.0

# The Mathematical Form of Duty Correspondence in Field Concretization

Section 1  
Purpose

### 1.1 What Problem This System Solves

On declared host instances, *The General Form of Field Concretization* v1.0 set up the necessary structure for a Sedimentation-Field to hold: three duties—Trace-Response set, organizational participation and historical dynamics—that take one and the same Trace-Response set as argument. *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* v1.0 wrote these duties as atoms over mechanism witnesses, and *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0, *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0 and other papers evaluated these atoms on models of the event-gating class and of the linear own-position families respectively. What has not been answered is a question between instances: between two host models with different mechanisms, which correspondence conditions, each verifiable independently on the mechanism laws, allow the duty atoms, qualification readings and dynamics conclusions of one side to be transferred to the other; which condition, when it fails, makes the transfer fail, and at which atom the failure lands; and what further conditions the transfer of dynamics conclusions (Differential Accumulation D, Continuation Bias B\_m, Familiar-Route Locking F) requires.  
This question cannot be answered by “the readings on both sides are the same”: equal readings can be a coincidence of values with no common basis. Nor can it be answered by “the state equations are the same” or “the actual runs correspond beat by beat”: the atoms of the historical-dynamics duty depend on counterfactual transitions, read structure and effectiveness thresholds, and correspondence of actual runs does not suffice to determine them. On declared model instances and mapping classes, this system gives correspondence conditions, preservation theorems, failure boundaries and witnesses, and on the main pair Instance P ↔ Family E of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* it characterizes the exact domains of preservation and of non-preservation.

### 1.2 The Structure Formalized

The structure formalized is  
　　S₁₅ (main) = the formal master formula Fld(χ) ⇒ ∃T \[TraceSet ∧ Part ∧ HDyn\] of *The General Form of Field Concretization* v1.0 and its expansion: q, r, Ident, HistEv, Cond, Act, HistCond (five modes of determination: occurrence, timing, position, strength, direction), the process-identification rule, carrier attribution, the convergence-round lemma, the in-boundary self-feedback type;  
　　S₁₅ (aux) (used only for the transfer of dynamics) = D, B\_m, B\_m^q, F of *The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics* v1.2 and their definitional constituents, as represented on hosts by *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0.  
For S₁₅, on declared instance pairs and mapping classes, this system establishes the formal system S₁₅ ⟼ M₁₅: five kinds of correspondence maps, correspondence conditions, preservation relations, three-level issuance and read-back rules. The Form kind is Mathematical Form, within the mathematical group established by *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*. Entry conditions: S₁₅ (main) has been established by the proof principal form *The General Form of Field Concretization* v1.0; S₁₅ (aux) has been conditionally established by *The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics* v1.2 (its minimality and completeness are unproved; this system uses only the definitions and the proved relations); every component has stable terminology. M₁₅ takes over from *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* v1.0 the two layers R and F, the witness-preserving embedding J, the three-valued evidence domain and the read-back regime; wherever the Trace-Responses determine the occurrence, timing or position of an action, evaluation uses R-layer witnesses (8.2 (6) of that paper).  
The main output of M₁₅ consists of theorems of the form “correspondence conditions ⇒ atom preservation” together with their failure boundaries. It is not an adjudication of the level of equivalence between two models (that is the content of the comparative meta-form), nor a qualification verdict on any instance. The antecedents of the preservation theorems contain no equality of atom values or of qualification values; “if all load-bearing atoms are preserved, qualification is preserved” is a definitional corollary (Theorem 15-14) and is not counted as an increment of this system.

### 1.3 Boundary

Object domain: the main pair, frozen SNN Instance P (s1 parameters; that is, the frozen instance v2, whose program snn\_core.py is carried in Appendix A.1 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0, Appendix A.1 of this paper being identical to it character for character), taken as the R-layer declared instance of Definitions 4–7 of Section 3 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0 and as Instance P of 7.5 of that paper ↔ the encoding J(χ\_E) of Family E of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0; the boundary pair, Family A ↔ Family E (Proposition 5 of the same paper); the family-level theorems additionally cover the members of the listed mapping classes that satisfy the antecedents. Parameters and states are rational, windows are finite and inputs are deterministic. The conclusions are model conclusions (strength A); the conclusions on the SNN belong to the algorithmic level of the program host and imply nothing about neuromorphic hardware or biological tissue; the conclusions on Families A and E hold only within the idealizations and constitutive antecedents of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*. Transfer statements on the real-host side are at most conditional predictions (B) and must list the unverified correspondence conditions; this system issues no empirical certification (C). Distinguishability under observation protocols belongs to *The Indistinguishability Form of Field Concretization* and *The Discrimination Form of Sedimentation Dynamics*; composition of records with the same object, the same T and the same process inventory but different runs, regimes or measurements belongs to *The Adjudication Form of Material Field Concretization* v1.0, 5.8.

Section 2  
Core Thesis

### 2.1 Central Content

The central content this system must express precisely is this: the transfer of duty atoms between instances is a function of counterfactual transitions, read and route structure, effectiveness thresholds and the correspondence protocol, not a function of readings or of actual runs.  
Six requirements follow. First, Theorem 15-3 uses event-wise conjugacy on the counterfactual domain as its sufficient condition and preserves the mechanism readings of the historical-dynamics atoms; beat-by-beat correspondence of actual runs, conjugacy of state transitions and graph isomorphism cannot replace it (Theorem 15-4); it is not a necessary condition for every route to preservation (the domain partition on fixed pure classes has a separate proof, Theorem 15-15). Second, preservation of certified three-valued readings (including 0 and ⊥) additionally requires that the negative-determination declarations of the two sides correspond; without that correspondence only positive certificates transfer (Theorem 15-3 (2)). Third, aggregation maps take whole-step commutation as antecedent; the F layer books only effective components, and the same-branch condition CQ is a sufficient, not a necessary, condition for aggregation to commute with the effectiveness threshold (Lemma 15-1a). Fourth, transfer is issued fibre by fibre: when a source atom takes two values on a fibre, that fibre has no recovery rule, while other fibres and condition sets that exclude it are unaffected (P15-6, Theorem 15-8); on pure classes with fixed protocol parameters and proved injectivity, the domain of conditional preservation is characterized by fibre protocol data, and positive determinations are further restricted to the decidable subdomain without clipping; substantive transfer, in which both values occur, is issued only for classes with formation count n ≤ 23, and on classes with n ≥ 24 the positive-determination domain is empty (Theorem 15-15). Fifth, a target-side determined Pres⁺ holds without constituting substantive transfer and supplies no information about the source side (D15-8, Theorem 15-9). Sixth, preservation at the formal layer does not cross over to the physical layer (Theorem 15-13).

### 2.2 Structure Inherited from Upstream

*The General Form of Field Concretization* v1.0: the declared instance χ = (P, L, B, W); the active-concretization stipulation; the formal master formula of TraceSet, Part and HDyn and its read-back; the delimitation of Cond, Act and HistCond (HistCond: the action of a process on Org(T)—its occurrence, timing, position, strength or direction—is determined in part, through an actual material route, by the Trace-Responses already left by T; symbols and types of Section 5 of that paper); the process-identification rule (mechanism processes are the unit; decomposable processes must not be merged into a composite process); the convergence-round lemma; the in-boundary self-feedback type of Section 7.  
*The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* v1.0: D1, the three-valued domain and strong Kleene operations; D4–D9, R-layer event witnesses, K\_t, RStep, joint events; D10–D20, the F layer (D12 linear laws, D15 functional support, D16 formation records and out-of-boundary causes, D17 available positions, D18 event atoms, D19 qualification, D20 domain); 4.2, the F layer books only effective components; D21, the witness-preserving embedding; Section 7, relation properties; 8.2, read-back antecedents; Theorem 1 (mechanism witnesses are incompressible), Theorem 2 (witness-preserving embedding and preservation of qualification), Theorem 5 (conditional read-back); 13.2, unanswered questions.  
*The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0: Definitions 1–3, the event-gating class 𝒦\_g, and Proposition 1 (the frozen SNN ∈ 𝒦\_g); Definitions 4–7, R-layer declared instances; Lemmas R1–R3; 3.3, the declared intervention domain 𝒟₁ (single-beat joint settings of the available T positions within \[b, hi\]); 3.6, Proposition N1, Lemma Q, Propositions N2 and N2′; 4.2, the three tiers of η; 4.3, criterion PS and Clause 0; Lemma 3.2; Lemma C; Theorem B2; 7.5, Instances P and N and the controls.  
*The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0: the correspondence obligations C-C; 4.2 Family A, 4.3 Family E; 5.2, 5.3; Lemma 1 (projection omission); Proposition 4; the failure verdicts; Proposition 5 (isomorphism of the Structural-Position encodings and identity of readings) and the four-layer separation; 7.2, item-by-item mechanism-level differences.  
*The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0: 2.4, the four hosts; D4, the bifurcation reading; D7–D10, reading types and Lead\_q; D14, R-shift and the valid domain C\_leg; D15, equal-depth identity; D16; D17, the effective relaxation rate; 4.1; Lemma M1; Theorems C-I, W-2, S-PAR, S-S1, J-1, W-1, R-B and Proposition DG-1; 11.2, 11.5 (3) (the depression component reads the weight in step fashion through gating), 11.6.  
*The Adjudication Form of Material Field Concretization* v1.0: 5.8, cross-record composition (Comp\_Γ, V\_Γ, M2-1–M2-3) and its demarcation from cross-object correspondence.  
*The Necessity Form of Field-Concretization Qualification* v1.0: Theorem 14-1⁺ and the condition group C\_LS⁺, Theorem 14-7 (used only for comparison and as family-level instances of this system's gating pivot condition); Lemma 14-L and the structural observation on the linear own-position family.

### 2.3 Working Input

　　𝕀₁₅ = ( 𝔐₁, 𝔐₂, Φ, 𝒞, S, A )  
𝔐₁, 𝔐₂ are the declared model instances (D15-1); Φ consists of the five kinds of maps together with the transducer for out-of-boundary signals (D15-4, D15-5); 𝒞 is the declared mapping class and instance class; S is the set of correspondence conditions used (D15-6); A is the atom or dynamics conclusion under discussion.

### 2.4 Instance Pairs

Main pair. 𝔐₁ = Instance P: s1 parameters (4 entries, 2 gating units, 8 synapses; λ = 3/4, θ = 1, v\_reset = 0, d\_x = d\_y = 1/2, A₊ = 1/8, A₋ = 1/16, ρ = 0, baseline b = 1/4, \[lo, hi\] = \[0, 2\]); the declared history is formation (A + teaching (1, 0), rest, rest) × 6, rest for 8 beats, and the window W = beats 26–43 as (A, rest, rest, B, rest, rest) × 3; T₁ = {w00, w10}, ε₁ = 1/100. 𝔐₂ = the encoding of Family E: two positions z1, z2; the formation letter pair injects an out-of-boundary pulse a = 2 into z1; under the drive letter drive each position is written b + k·z (b = k = 1/10); every beat decays by −γ·z (γ = 1/10); ε₂ = 1/1000; T₂ = {z1}.  
Boundary pair. Family A ↔ Family E, with the renaming of Proposition 5 of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*, seed ↔ pair, flow ↔ drive, write ↔ hebb, relax ↔ decay, as the map.  
Notation: the conductance coefficient κ of the constitutive law of Family A and the continuation tendency κ share a name but differ in meaning; this system does not use the latter. “Equal values” means only that the readings on both sides are the same; it does not signify certification.

Section 3  
Basic Objects and the Definition Sequence

### 3.1 The Definition Sequence

D15-1　Object class ℛ. A finite-dimensional deterministic R-layer declared instance 𝔐 = (χ, Ξ, A, β, 𝒦, h, o₀, T, W, ε, 𝒟, 𝒮): Ξ is the set of Structural Positions, A the set of auxiliary nodes, β: Ξ ∪ A → {B, outside}; 𝒦 is the set of process kinds, each kind k carrying a duty class role(k) ∈ {+write, −depression, −relaxation, read-out, formation, auxiliary}, a carrier car(k), a target tgt(k), an occurrence predicate occ\_k(o, σ), a component c\_k(o, σ) and a read set reads\_k(σ); h is the declared letter history and o₀ the declared initial state; 𝒟 is the declared intervention domain (of 𝒟₁ type); 𝒮 is the set of other declared interventions (switches, route cuts, erasure, I\_m). Transitions have two semantics: R models, o′(n) = o(n) + Σ\_{tgt(e)=n} c\_e (the members of 𝒦\_g are of this kind, Definition 6 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*); F models book only effective components Q(c) = c·\[\|c\| \> ε\] (4.2 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*). ℛ contains the members of 𝒦\_g and the images under D21 of F models of the linear own-position families (Families A and E via J of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*).  
D15-2　Common reading layer and atoms. The atoms of both sides are always evaluated on R-layer witnesses: a(e) = \[\|c\_e\| \> ε\]; η(e) by the three tiers of 4.2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* (1: FD\_𝒟 has an implemented witness and Path\_act = 1; 0(a): Path\_slot = 0; 0(b): FD\_𝒟 = 0, fully adjudicated, carrying B\_FD; ⊥ with a reason code); κ likewise; Part and HDyn by D8 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*; q by z \> ε at window end and the formation record; r: on 𝒦\_g by criterion PS, on F models by D16 and D19 (effective change by an out-of-boundary pulse, i.e., Clause 0); Trace = q ∧ r ∧ Ident ∧ HistEv; Q; in-boundary self-feedback ι. The 0s and some of the ⊥s in verdicts depend on negative-determination declarations (intervention domain, completeness of the inventory, B\_FD, the clipping individuation reading); a 1 depends only on a positive certificate; clipping individuation follows the upstream rule that the two readings must agree, and disagreement is recorded as ⊥\_Ind.  
D15-3　Modes of determination. The law-level mode of determination Md^law(k) ⊆ {occurrence, timing, position, strength, direction}: “occurrence” ∈ Md^law if and only if occ\_k reads a node that can change with T; “strength” if and only if c\_k reads such a node; “direction” if and only if the sign of c\_k can change with it; in 𝒦\_g and in the linear own-position families the timing of events and the position of action are determined by the letters and the declaration, so “timing” and “position” belong to Md^law for no kind. The actual mode of determination Md(e) is decided by whether some setting in 𝒟 flips the occurrence of e, keeps e occurring but changes its component, or changes the sign of its component, respectively. Law types are recorded as well: gated step type, linear type, constant type, pulse. Kind by kind: SNN PLUS {occurrence}, gated step (P⁺ = A₊x′ᵢ reads only the eligibility trace); MINUS {strength}, gated step (−A₋(d·y\_j + g\_j), reading the weight through same-beat gating); SYN {strength}, linear (c = w\_ij); RELAX {strength}, linear (absent in s1, where ρ = 0); Family E hebb {strength}, linear b + k·z; decay {strength}, linear −γ·z; pair, pulse.  
D15-4　The five kinds of maps and their validity conditions.  
　φ\_Ξ: position-map type—a partial injection Ξ₁ ⇀ Ξ₂ with a depth scale α\_x \> 0 for each position (z₂ = α\_x·z₁); aggregation type—maps a declared family of paths {P} to positions of Ξ₂, z₂ ↔ α·Agg\_P(z₁). Valid: Agg(0) = 0, monotone nondecreasing in each coordinate, T₂ = φ\_Ξ(T₁) and T₁ a union of whole paths. Aggregation is either linear (Agg\_P(z) = Σ\_{x∈P} a\_x z\_x, a\_x \> 0; the mean has a\_x = 1/\|P\|) or nonlinear (such as the bottleneck min); what the preservation theorems use is whole-step commutation MC2^step (D15-6); for linear aggregation Lemma 15-1a gives a law-level sufficient condition for it, while nonlinear aggregation must verify MC2^step case by case and otherwise lies outside the theorems of this system. There is also an auxiliary map φ\_A: A₁ ⇀ A₂ (possibly empty).  
　φ\_E: the kind relation R\_E ⊆ 𝒦₁ × (𝒦₂ ∪ {∅}). Valid: duty class preserved (+ ↔ +, − ↔ −, read-out ↔ read-out, formation ↔ formation); targets compatible (tgt₂ = φ\_Ξ(tgt₁), or both auxiliary with φ\_A defined). Event summation rule: in the one-to-one case, events correspond event by event; in the many-to-one case (one k₂ corresponding to several (k₁, x) acting on the positions of one path), the target component corresponds to the weighted sum Σ a\_x c\_{k₁,x} of the source components, and the target event occurs if and only if at least one preimage occurs.  
　φ\_B: the map of carriers and of the in-boundary attribution of nodes, total on the domain of R\_E and on the nodes of route chains.  
　φ\_t: a beat map (strictly increasing, W₁ bijective onto W₂, entry beats to entry beats, event nodes t + ½ ↦ φ\_t(t) + ½) or a block map (a monotone surjection with block length L; the events within a block are merged into a block event, which changes the individuation of events and must be declared).  
　φ\_I: the map of declared interventions, by type (setting ↦ setting, switch ↦ switch or cut, erasure ↦ erasure, I\_m ↦ I\_m), mapping valid interventions to valid interventions.  
D15-5　Map of out-of-boundary signals: the frozen transducer. The correspondence of out-of-boundary signals is a fixed sequence function τ: Σ₁\* → Σ₂\*, given by a finite-state Mealy machine (state f, initial value f₀, beat-wise output τ\_out(f, σ) and transition τ\_next(f, σ)); the image history h₂ = τ(h₁) corresponds beat by beat. A static letter function is the special case with a single state. τ is frozen once declared. The cascade of two transducers is again a transducer (with the product of the states). The τ of the main pair: a one-bit state f with initial value 0; when the letter is exactly (A, teaching (1, 0)), f = 0 outputs pair and sets f = 1, and f = 1 outputs rest; all other letters with entries 0 and 1 both open (A, AB, any teaching current) output drive; B, rest and letters with teaching current only output rest.  
D15-6　Correspondence conditions (objects of verification: laws, process tables, route chains, intervention switches; atom values are not an object of verification).  
　MC1　Event correspondence. MC1-W: R\_E, restricted to the kinds acting on T within the window, is a correspondence preserving duty class, carrier and target, and is surjective onto the kinds of the other side acting on T₂. MC1-F: the carrier attributions of formation and trace-leaving events correspond. MC1-P: the kinds whose carrier is in B, whose read set contains positions of T and whose target is not in T have images on the other side. MC1 further comes in an injective type and a bijective type.  
　MC2　Reads and modes of determination. MC2-r: when (k₁, k₂) ∈ R\_E, the read sets map onto each other under the beat-wise correspondence of φ\_Ξ ∪ φ\_A ∪ τ (the auxiliary quantities, non-T positions and out-of-boundary components read must have images). MC2-m: Md^law(k₁) = Md^law(k₂), or an explicitly declared conversion between modes together with its additional conditions. MC2⁺(Δ): event-wise conjugacy on the state domain Δ—the occurrence predicates of corresponding kinds take equal values and the components satisfy c₂ = α·c₁ (many-to-one by the summation rule). Δ\_act is the set of states of the actual trajectory, Δ\_𝒟 the states reachable from actual states by settings in 𝒟, Δ\_I the states reachable by a declared intervention I. MC2⁺ is only a numerical conjugacy; it contains neither equality of read sets nor preservation and reflection of graph structure.  
　MC2^step(Δ)　Whole-step commutation: for o ∈ Δ and corresponding letters, Φ(K₁(o, σ)) = K₂(Φ(o), σ′) holds on the mapped coordinates (K is the one-beat transition, under the respective R or F semantics; σ′ is the corresponding output of τ).  
　MC-G　Read, time and graph structure: (G1) φ\_t is strictly increasing and preserves the timing of event nodes; (G2) on the relevant node set (the nodes forward-reachable on the slot hypergraph from the available nodes of T up to window end, together with the nodes involved in their reads and writes), the node map is bijective and preserves in-boundary attribution; (G3) on the relevant event kinds R\_E is bijective, and read edges, write edges and persistence edges correspond bijectively, i.e., the relevant slot hypergraphs of the two sides are isomorphic and the isomorphism preserves time.  
　MC3　Route correspondence: the declared route chains of the corresponding kinds acting on T (with the carrier of the read position as head of the chain, each segment a declared connection, ending at t + ½) correspond node by node, every node lies in B, and timing is strictly forward. MC3ˢ (used only for the source): on the chain from an out-of-boundary signal component to the effective change on T, start maps to start and end to end, and intermediate nodes in B may be compressed.  
　MC4　Intervention commutation: for every declared intervention I, (i) at the state level, Φ(I(o)) = φ\_I(I)(Φ(o)); (ii) at the process level, the set of kinds that I prevents from occurring is mapped by R\_E to the set of kinds that φ\_I(I) prevents from occurring, and the sets of kinds for which it only makes the route certification 0 correspond likewise (“changes the dynamics” and “changes only the certification” must not be mapped to each other); (iii) MC4-𝒟: φ\_I restricted to 𝒟₁ is a bijection onto 𝒟₂ and satisfies (i).  
　MC5　Resolution and timescale: MC5-ε: ε₂ = α·ε₁; MC5-τ: φ\_t realizes, on the beats of the other side, the reading of each side's timescale antecedents.  
　H-corr (declared correspondence, not a correspondence condition): the entry-beat states correspond on the mapped coordinates; the image history h₂ = τ(h₁); the formation events correspond.  
　ND (correspondence of negative-determination declarations, at the declaration level): the declarations consumed by negative determinations on the two sides are identical item by item and correspond under Φ—antecedents such as the declared intervention domain, completeness of the inventory and faithfulness of records (𝔅₀ of Theorem D2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*), together with whether the object-level reading B\_FD\[𝒟₁\] of tier (b) is declared; the clipping individuation readings are the same. ND is not implied by any mechanism conjugacy (Appendix B.7, Part 2: identical models, with B\_FD declared on one side and not on the other).  
D15-7　Preservation. Preservation has two kinds of object: mechanism readings (predicates such as FD, Path and Act, determined by the laws and the declared intervention domain, taking 0, 1) and certified three-valued readings (the verdicts on η, κ, Part, HDyn, which can be ⊥ and depend on negative-determination declarations). For an atom A: Pres⁺\_Φ(A): A₁ = 1 ⇒ A₂ = 1; Pres⁻\_Φ(A): A₁ = 0 ⇒ A₂ = 0; Pres\_Φ(A): A₁ ∈ {0, 1} ⇒ A₂ = A₁. ⊥ does not transfer (a ⊥ on one side places no constraint on the other and is not written as 0). “The condition set S certifies the preservation of A on 𝒞” means: Pres holds on every (𝔐₁, 𝔐₂, Φ) in 𝒞 that satisfies S.  
D15-8　Substantive transfer and target-side determination. A Pres(A) certified by S on 𝒞 is called substantive if and only if both values of A₂ (and of A₁) occur on the pairs in 𝒞 that satisfy S; if A₂ takes one and the same value on every pair satisfying S, determined by the conditions on 𝔐₂ in S alone, it is called target-side determined—it holds but brings no information from 𝔐₁.  
D15-9　Fibres. Fix a frozen Φ (including τ) and a declared class 𝒞₁ of 𝔐₁. Fibre protocol data = (the laws via Φ, the values of the entry state on the mapped coordinates, the image history τ(h₁), the window W, and the protocol parameters fixed by the declared class); the fibre over a target image declaration = all declarations in 𝒞₁ whose fibre protocol data equal it. A fibre is called pure for the atom A₁ if A₁ takes a single value within it, and ambiguous otherwise. On a pure fibre there is a correct recovery rule taking fibre protocol data as input; on an ambiguous fibre there is none. Purity is relative to the declared class: shrinking or fixing protocol parameters can make an ambiguous fibre pure.  
D15-10　Gating pivot condition GS(t, j). At beat t and gating unit j: after the positions of T that are read by j in this beat and are available at t are set back to baseline, the gate argument vt\_j \< θ\_j; moreover θ\_j ≤ the actual vt\_j, and the set of positions set back is nonempty. If exactly vt\_j = θ\_j after setting back, the gate is still open and GS does not hold.  
D15-11　Gate-argument conjugacy TP. When both sides are occurrence-type events and the gate arguments are affine in same-beat settings: there is a positive constant c such that vt₂(φ\_I σ) − θ₂ = c·(vt₁(σ) − θ₁) for every same-beat setting in 𝒟₁. The non-degeneracy condition for a ≥ gate to be flippable under same-beat settings is min\_σ vt \< θ ≤ max\_σ vt. Event identity follows Definition 5 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*: PLUS takes occurrence in this beat as the event; when the amplitude is 0 (A₊ = 0), a change of occurrence is still a change in the sense of FD, but a = 0 and it does not bear HDyn.  
D15-12　Three-level issuance: Level 1, model-local correspondence; Level 2, common conditions of a restricted family; Level 3, cross-instance conclusions (Section 8).

### 3.2 Map Declaration for the Main Pair

φ\_Ξ: path aggregation of the bifurcation reading (path 0 = {w00, w10} ↦ z1, path 1 = {w01, w11} ↦ z2, Depth = the mean of z within the path, α = 1; α = 1/10 is also reported, to separate out MC5-ε). R\_E: PLUS ↦ hebb, MINUS ↦ decay (duty class “negative component”), formation write ↦ pair; SYN, LEAK, RESET, YIN, YDEC, XDEC, XIN, INJ, CLIP have no image. φ\_t: the identity beat map. τ: D15-5. φ\_I: bap off ↦ cut(N→syn); plastic off ↦ freeze (neither hebb nor decay runs); erasure (w ← b) ↦ z ← 0; settings in 𝒟₁ ↦ path-mean settings; I\_m ↦ I\_m (path level). The fibre protocol data do not include entries 2 and 3, or teaching currents other than that of the formation letter. The main image history τ(h₁) is physically valid (receptor-pool check passed); the image history of the static letter function (formation letter always ↦ pair) is also reported: its physical check fails, and its atoms are the same (Appendix B.2, Part 3).

Section 4  
Equations and Criteria  
4.1 Event Summation and Whole-Step Commutation  
One-to-one: c₂(Φo, σ′) = α·c₁(o, σ), occ₂ = occ₁. Many-to-one (linear aggregation with coefficients a\_x): c\_{k₂}(Φo, σ′) = α·Σ\_{(k₁,x)∈R\_E⁻¹(k₂)} a\_x c\_{k₁,x}(o, σ), and k₂ occurs if and only if some preimage occurs. The whole-step update of an R model is Agg(o + Σc); that of an F model is Agg(o + Σ Q₁(c)), with target o₂ + Σ Q₂(c₂).  
4.2 The Same-Branch Condition CQ (F Models)  
For the family of preimages {c\_x} of every target event: the aggregation coefficients are convex weights (Σ\_{x∈P} a\_x = 1); either all \|c\_x\| ≤ ε₁ (equality included), or all \|c\_x\| \> ε₁ with the same sign; and ε₂ = α·ε₁. Under CQ, Q₂(α Σ a\_x c\_x) = α Σ a\_x Q₁(c\_x) (Lemma 15-1a).

### 4.3 Fibre Criteria

Ambiguous: there are a fibre Fb and two declarations within Fb on which A₁ takes different values. Pure: Fb contains only one declaration, or A₁ is constant within Fb. Target-side determined: on every pair satisfying S, A₂ is given by the conditions on the 𝔐₂ side alone.  
4.4 Domain-Partition Predicates for the Pure Classes of the Main Pair  
On the class 𝒞₁ᵖ(n) with fixed formation count n (Theorem 15-15): Π\_I—there is an (A, 0) beat t within the window such that GS(t, 0) holds and z1 is available at t in the image, and moreover NC\_W holds: on every beat within the window the T positions have no clipping event (the pre-clip sum lies in \[lo, hi\]); Π\_II—the window is all rest and in the image γ·z1(t) ≤ ε₂ for every t ∈ W; Π\_III—the window is all rest and for some t ∈ W, γ·z1(t) \> ε₂ with z1 available; Π\_IV⁰—the window has an (A, 0) beat and output 0 does not fire on any beat within the window; Π\_IV\*—everything else (including the cases that satisfy the first three items of Π\_I but not NC\_W, recorded as Π\_sat and listed separately).

### 4.5 Effective Action on the Target Side

Eff₂ := within the window there is a bearing event e₂ acting on z1 such that z1 is available at t(e₂) and \|c(e₂)\| \> ε₂.

Section 5  
Running and Solving

### 5.1 Law-Level Verification

The law-level sub-items of MC1–MC5 are verified item by item on the process tables (Appendix A.10, mc\_check): duty classes, carriers, targets, auxiliary quantities read, law-level modes of determination, law types, node maps of route chains, the disabled sets and cut sets of interventions, and the scaling relation of ε.

### 5.2 Atom Evaluation

On the SNN side, Part and HDyn use the exact 𝒟₁ adjudicator of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0 (Appendix A.6); the same-beat removal certification of q and r and the read-out-class κ use the lightweight evaluation of Appendix A.10; on the side of Families A and E, the F-layer atoms are evaluated by D16–D19 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* (Appendix A.10, lf\_eval), and the intervention readings are additionally verified on finite settings (lf\_fd).  
5.3 Verification of Whole-Step Commutation and CQ  
Whole-step commutation of linear and bottleneck aggregation is verified exactly, beat by beat, on split sources (split\_step, wu\_mech\_step); commutation of F-layer effective booking with weighted sums is verified with Qe and cq\_condition (Appendix A.10).

### 5.4 Fibres and Domain Partition

The transducer τ (Appendix A.10, tau) gives the image histories; injectivity of fibres is checked case by case within the classes of fixed n; the partition predicates are computed from the source declaration (recovered from the fibre protocol data) and the image history.

### 5.5 Solvability

All objects are finite-dimensional, deterministic models with rational data and finite windows: each beat has a unique successor (in Theorem 3 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*, the number of successors per beat is 1), but the state set is in general infinite (for Family E resting after pair, the values z1 = 2·(9/10)^m are pairwise distinct, Appendix B.7, Part 4). Three kinds of evaluation are kept apart: (1) finite-trajectory evaluation—law-level verification on a given declared history, atom evaluation, beat-by-beat checks of whole-step commutation and fibre checks finish in finitely many steps, and the cost of the adjudicator grows exponentially with window length in the worst case (Theorem D1 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*); (2) verification on finite settings and finite samples—grids, enumeration and spot checks, which give only finite results; (3) proofs of domain-wide symbolic conditions—given by the theorems of the main text, not by running programs. General verification programs for MC2^step(Δ\_𝒟) and MC-G (symbolic execution plus linear feasibility) are not implemented, and this system runs no such uniform algorithm (U15-12).

Section 6  
Coupling Relations  
6.1 With *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* v1.0  
This system sets up maps between instances on that paper's two layers R and F and on J; when 𝔐₂ = J(𝔐₁) and Φ = J, Theorem 15-1 is its Theorem 2 (1) (2). Its Theorem 1 (the same π but different Act, HistCond) is, in this system's order of mapping classes, the witness of 𝒞\_π ⊋ 𝒞\_run; Theorem 15-4 gives a stronger form (correspondence of actual runs still does not suffice). The read-back antecedents of 8.2 are not supplied by transfer (RB15-1). For the unanswered question of 13.2 on a formal predicate of in-boundary self-feedback, this system relies on Theorem B2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* on 𝒦\_g, and on 5.2 of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* on F models.  
6.2 With *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0  
The R-layer declared instances and the adjudicator for the SNN are taken from Definitions 4–7 and the appendix programs of that paper; GS is a sufficient condition for same-beat positive determination of the type of its Lemma 3.2 (setting only available positions). Theorem 15-10 (1), under the jump condition, excludes the super-beat encoding route not covered by 3.6 of that paper.  
6.3 With *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0  
The physical mechanism models and encodings of Families A and E follow its Section 4 and C-C; the map of the boundary pair takes the renaming of Proposition 5; Theorem 15-13 (2) inherits the four-layer separation: isomorphism of encodings does not read back physical validity, resource feasibility or encoding–mechanism correspondence.  
6.4 With *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0  
The additional conditions DT1–DT5 for the transfer of dynamics (Theorem 15-12) cite D14–D17, Lemma M1 and Theorems C-I, W-2, S-PAR, J-1, W-1, R-B of that paper; the case in which erasure is I\_min cites its 11.6.  
6.5 With *The Adjudication Form of Material Field Concretization* v1.0  
Its V\_Γ of 5.8 (atom transfer under the same law kind, the same read multiset and parameters within tolerance) amounts to a tolerance version of this system's MC2⁺ for the same law kind; its “formation atoms do not transfer” is compatible with MC1-F and Theorem 15-7 of this system. Composition of records with the same object, the same T and the same process inventory belongs to that paper; everything else belongs to this system.  
6.6 With *The Necessity Form of Field-Concretization Qualification* v1.0  
The pivot of C\_LS⁺ and Theorem 14-1⁺ is a family-level instance of GS on the SNN parameter family; Lemma 14-L and the structural observation on the linear own-position family are the basis of Theorem 15-9 (1); this system does not reprove their conclusions.

Section 7  
Relation Properties  
P15-1　Composition. Valid maps can be composed: position maps compose to position maps; linear aggregations compose to linear aggregations (coefficients multiplied and summed); a composition of nonlinear aggregations yields only a valid declaration, and its whole-step commutation must be checked separately; R\_E composes as a relation, preserving duty class; compositions of beat maps are strictly increasing, and compositions of block maps are block maps; transducers cascade to transducers; φ\_I composes by type. MC1 (bijective part), MC2-r, MC2-m, MC2⁺ (α multiplied), MC2^step (pasting of commuting squares), MC-G (composition of isomorphisms), MC3 (composition of chain maps), MC4 (pasting of commuting squares) and MC5-ε (ε₃ = α₂α₁ε₁) are closed under composition; for F models, CQ must be verified layer by layer under composition. Pres⁺, Pres⁻ and Pres are transitive under composition. Witness: Family A → Family E → Family E\_α (α ∈ {2, 1/3}), 1704 groups with identical atoms and trajectories scaled by α (Appendix B.3, Part 9). \[proved; finite verification\]  
P15-2　Inversion and unidirectionality. When every component is bijective and the conditions hold, Φ⁻¹ is valid and satisfies the corresponding conditions (with α⁻¹). Preservation certified by a condition set S yields reverse preservation by inversion only when S is symmetric: MC1 of injective type certifies Pres⁺(HDyn) but not Pres⁻(HDyn). Witness: Family E (γ = 0) against Family E, φ\_E: hebb ↦ hebb (decay has no preimage), history (pair, rest, rest): HDyn₁ = 0, HDyn₂ = 1 (Appendix B.3, Part 8). The preservation relation is not symmetric. \[proved; exact witness\]  
P15-3　Order of condition sets. The subsets of the sub-items of the correspondence conditions are partially ordered by inclusion (a Boolean lattice), not totally ordered; “S certifies the preservation of A” is upward closed in S; if minimal condition sets exist, they form an antichain. For no atom has the full set of antecedents been shown irredundant by deleting items; the minimality of no condition set has been proved. \[proved\]  
P15-4　Order of mapping classes. Every class is defined under the same background constraints: Φ valid, H-corr, the required closures and valid inputs (declared histories and interventions within their respective domains). Recursively: 𝒞\_π := background constraints ∩ {the state trajectories correspond beat by beat on the mapped coordinates, D22 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*}; 𝒞\_run := 𝒞\_π ∩ {events and components correspond beat by beat}; 𝒞\_cf := 𝒞\_run ∩ {MC2⁺(Δ\_𝒟) ∧ MC-G ∧ MC3 ∧ MC4-𝒟}; 𝒞\_enc := 𝒞\_cf ∩ {the F encodings are identical item by item up to renaming and scale, of the type of Proposition 5 of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*}. Hence 𝒞\_π ⊋ 𝒞\_run ⊋ 𝒞\_cf ⊋ 𝒞\_enc, a preorder by inclusion of conditions. Witnesses of strictness: π versus run—the Act example of Theorem 1 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* (inherited); run versus cf—Theorem 15-4 (A) (B); cf versus enc—scale conjugacy on 𝒦\_g (Instances P and P\_α belong to 𝒞\_cf, but instances of 𝒦\_g have no exact F encoding, Proposition N2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*). \[proved; witnesses\]  
P15-5　“𝔐₁ can be transferred to 𝔐₂ via S” as a relation between instances: reflexive (identity Φ), transitive (P15-1), not symmetric (P15-2), not antisymmetric (two scale-conjugate instances can be transferred to each other and yet are different); it is a preorder. \[proved\]  
P15-6　Quantifiers for ambiguous fibres. If A₁ takes two values on a fibre Fb, then: (1) there is no recovery rule taking fibre protocol data as input that is correct everywhere on Fb; (2) if A₂ = 1 on Fb, then any condition set that certifies “A₂ = 1 ⇒ A₁ = 1” and has only fibre protocol data as antecedents must fail on Fb; if A₂ = 0 on Fb, this positive implication holds vacuously and does not fail because the source takes two values; for Pres (recovery of values), whatever value A₂ takes, a condition set certifying Pres must exclude Fb. This property does not rule out condition sets that exclude Fb, does not rule out transfer on other fibres, and does not concern other maps. Proof: the two declarations within Fb satisfy every antecedent that depends only on fibre protocol data, yet their values of A₁ differ. \[proved\]

Section 8  
Read-Back Rules

### 8.1 Direction

M → S: through the antecedents below, the preservation conclusions of this system read back relations between the components of S₁₅ on the two instances. S → M: whether the duties on two hosts are “the same bearing” in the general sense is not guaranteed by this system.  
8.2 Formal Conditions of the Three-Level Issuance  
Level 1 (model-local correspondence): the object is a declared (𝔐₁, 𝔐₂, Φ). Conditions: Φ is valid (D15-4, D15-5); the correspondence conditions used and H-corr are verified item by item on the laws, process tables, route chains and intervention switches of both sides; the aggregation type comes with verification of whole-step commutation, and F models with verification of CQ; all antecedents of the theorems invoked hold. Form of issue: “under Φ, S ⊢ Pres(A) (or Pres⁺), marked substantive or target-side determined, with the fibre or domain issued noted”. Strength A.  
Level 2 (common conditions of a restricted family): the object is a mapping class 𝒞 and a condition set S; S holds at family level for every member of 𝒞 (by law-level symbolic verification or by proof, not replaced by finite samples); the universal antecedents of the theorems are written out item by item. Strength A.  
Level 3 (cross-instance conclusions): chains composed from Levels 1 and 2 by P15-1. Both ends models: A. Either end a real host: at most B, and the following must be listed: the instance certificate or qualification study of the real host; the sub-items of the correspondence conditions not verified on the real host (process tables and individuation, read sets, route chains, process-level effects of interventions, resolution and timescale); and the read-back antecedents of 8.2 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* for the real host, with the means of breaching them. This system does not issue C.

### 8.3 Forward Read-Back (with Antecedents)

RB15-1　The A₂ obtained by transfer is only a formal reading of 𝔐₂; reading it back as a duty on χ₂ requires χ₂'s own antecedents of 8.2 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*, which transfer does not supply.  
RB15-2　Equal values not certified by a condition set are not transfer, and are not read as the duties on the two sides having a common basis.  
RB15-3　A target-side determined transfer is recorded only as “target-side determined” and does not serve as evidence about 𝔐₁.  
RB15-4　Non-transfer on an ambiguous fibre is not read as A₁ = 0 or ⊥, nor generalized to other fibres or other maps; A₁ is evaluated separately by the source-side criteria.  
RB15-5　Isomorphism of encodings or formal full preservation does not read back physical validity, resource feasibility or encoding–mechanism correspondence.  
RB15-6　Sameness of state equations or of actual runs is not read back as sameness of mechanism, source or carrier.  
RB15-7　The universal convention “for every valid m” for dynamics transfer is stated only with the B set C\_B, and requires \[min, max\] ∩ ℚ ⊆ C\_leg (D16 of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* and the corollary of Lemma M1).  
RB15-8　A target-side HDyn = 0 (as in a long rest window) holds only for the listed window and T, and is not read as a negative determination on the host.  
RB15-9　Transfer of certified three-valued readings requires ND; without ND, only positive certificates are read back (Pres⁺ of η = 1 and HDyn = 1); the reason codes of ⊥ are not issued in advance across instances, and a ⊥ on the undeclared side is not changed to 0.  
RB15-10　On fixed pure classes, verdicts under Π\_sat (clipping at T positions within the window) are not read back as 1; a conclusion under a single reading is not called the common upstream verdict; on classes with n ≥ 24 there is only conditional preservation of the zero value, which is read neither as substantive transfer nor as a negative determination.

### 8.4 Upper Bound of Read-Back

The conclusions of this system are formal conclusions between models. Without an instance certificate for the real host and mechanism evidence for the correspondence conditions, transfer does not constitute certification of duties on the real host; the lack of ready-made experiments is no reason to judge 0, to delete the object or to stop the research.
