Section 10  
Formal Realization Theorems  
The proofs in this section are all elementary: induction on beats, commuting squares, piecewise affine maps, comparison of step functions with continuous functions, finite enumeration. The programs perform only finite verification and supply exact witnesses; universal conclusions rest on the proofs. Each item states its antecedents, conclusion, proof, witnesses and scope.  
10.0 Lemma 15-0 (On the Linear Own-Position Family the Intervention Reading Equals the F-Layer HistCond)  
Let 𝔐 be an F model of the linear own-position family (M(a, b, k, γ, ε) of Proposition 5 (ii) of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*), let e be a write or relaxation event within the window acting on T position i, with i available at t(e), and let the declared intervention domain contain a non-degenerate interval of settings of i at t(e). Then the intervention reading η(e) = HistCond\_F(e).  
Proof: the law of e is c = b + k·s(i) (for relaxation b = 0, k = −γ), which reads only its own position. When k ≠ 0, a same-beat setting changes c, so FD = 1; the read edge lies on the actual event graph, and Path\_act takes the certification value of the route chain; Supp\_act = {i}, and both equal \[route certification = 1\]. When k = 0, a same-beat setting does not change c, and settings at earlier beats change s(i)(t) but c does not read s(i), so FD = 0; Supp\_act = ∅ and HistCond\_F = 0. ∎  
Limits: stated only for events whose position of action is available at t(e) (U15-6); it concerns only HistCond, not effective action—when the position is available and \|c\| ≤ ε, HistCond = 1 and Act = 0 (Theorem 15-9). For finite verification see Appendix B.2, Part 3. This lemma is the counterpart, for the intervention reading, of the structural observation on the linear own-position family in *The Necessity Form of Field-Concretization Qualification*, and is not counted as an increment.  
10.1 Theorem 15-1 (Run Correspondence) and Lemma 15-1a  
Theorem 15-1. Antecedents: Φ valid; MC1-W (bijective type) ∧ MC1-F ∧ MC2^step(Δ\_act) ∧ MC5-τ (beat map) ∧ H-corr. Conclusion: for every beat t, Φ(o₁(t)) = o₂(t) (on the mapped coordinates). If the event conditions of Lemma 15-1a hold as well, the occurring events correspond by the summation rule (c₂ = α·c₁ in the one-to-one case); this is written RC. Proof: induction on beats; the entry beat by H-corr, the induction step by MC2^step(Δ\_act). ∎ When 𝔐₂ = J(𝔐₁) and Φ = J, this is Theorem 2 (1) (2) of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*; it is not counted as a separate increment.  
Lemma 15-1a (a law-level sufficient condition for whole-step commutation). Let φ\_Ξ be a position map or a linear aggregation, and suppose that for o ∈ Δ: (i) the component of every target kind corresponds to the source components by the summation rule of 4.1, and a target kind occurs if and only if some preimage occurs; (ii) closure—every source event acting on mapped coordinates has an image, and every target kind acting on φ(T) or on read coordinates has a preimage; (iii) out-of-boundary pulses correspond by the same rule.  
　(R) branch: for R models, MC2^step(Δ) holds. Proof: the post-state of the mapped coordinates = the pre-state + the sum of all components; under linear aggregation Agg(o + Σc) = Agg(o) + Σ\_x a\_x Σc\_x, and by (i), (ii), (iii) the terms are equal one by one. ∎  
　(F) branch: for F models, assume further (iv) CQ (4.2). Then MC2^step(Δ) holds. Proof: we need Q₂(α Σ a\_x c\_x) = α Σ a\_x Q₁(c\_x). When all are ineffective, the right-hand side is 0, and on the left \|α Σ a\_x c\_x\| ≤ α·max\|c\_x\| ≤ αε₁ = ε₂ (convex weights), so the left-hand side is 0 as well; when all are effective with the same sign, \|Σ a\_x c\_x\| = Σ a\_x\|c\_x\| \> ε₁ (convex weights), so the left-hand side is not truncated, and the right-hand side is just α Σ a\_x c\_x. Once equality holds for each target event, the induction proceeds as in the (R) branch. ∎ For position maps, CQ requires only ε₂ = α·ε₁. CQ is a sufficient condition, not a necessary one: with c = (1/50, −1/50), the mean and ε = 1/100, CQ fails yet Q commutes with the mean (Appendix B.7, Part 3; U15-17).  
Witnesses and verification:  
　Nonlinear aggregation does not inherit event induction: two source positions start from (0, 0), and two constant writes add (1, 0) and (0, 1) respectively; one target position, to which the corresponding processes each add 1; occurrence and components agree event by event and the read sets are empty, yet the bottleneck aggregate of the source post-state is min(1, 1) = 1 while the target post-state is 2, so whole-step commutation fails (Appendix B.5, Part 1).  
　Positive control for linear aggregation: each synapse of Family E is split into two parallel sub-synapses with the same law (a four-position source), which path-mean aggregation maps to Family E; the formation pulse is split as (2, 2), (2, 0), (3, 1); over all histories of length 1–4 × 3 splits, 360 groups in all, whole-step commutation holds throughout under R semantics. Bottleneck aggregation of the same source, verified case by case, also holds throughout (in every beat the sub-synapses within a path undergo the same strictly increasing affine map, with which min commutes), whereas in the algebraic example of the preceding paragraph the two members undergo different events and commutation fails—hence nonlinear aggregation must be verified case by case (Appendix B.5, Part 1).  
　F branch: the mixed-threshold example c = (3/500, 7/500), path mean, ε = 1/100—the former ineffective, the latter effective, so CQ fails; the raw target component is exactly ε and is truncated, while the effective source mean is 7/1000, so they do not commute (Appendix B.6, Part 1). Convex weights (1/2, 1/2), (1/3, 2/3), (1/4, 3/4) × α ∈ {1, 2} × 81 pairs of components (subthreshold, at equality, suprathreshold, crossing the threshold after weighting), 486 cases in all: no case satisfies CQ and fails to commute. Positive control for F-layer splitting: with both sides booking by F truncation, all 360 cases of this group commute, 270 of which satisfy CQ throughout; outside CQ, commutation cannot be inferred from this sufficient condition alone (Appendix B.6, Part 1).  
\[proved; exact witnesses and finite verification\]

### 10.2 Theorem 15-2 (Act)

Antecedents: RC (one-to-one event correspondence) ∧ MC5-ε (F models additionally CQ, which holds automatically under position maps). Conclusion: Pres(a) for corresponding events. Proof: \|c₂\| = α\|c₁\|, ε₂ = αε₁. ∎ In the many-to-one case, the effectiveness of a target event is the effectiveness of a weighted sum and is not preserved source event by source event. Necessity of MC5-ε: an identity Φ that only changes ε from 1/100 to 1/1000 turns a of MINUS@5 (c = −3/512) of Instance N of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* from 0 to 1, and HDyn from 0 to 1 (Appendix B.3, Part 5). \[proved; exact witness\]  
10.3 Theorem 15-3 (Preservation of HistCond under Counterfactual Conjugacy: Mechanism Readings and Certified Three-Valued Readings Kept Apart)  
Antecedents (1): MC1-W (bijective type) ∧ MC2-r ∧ MC2⁺(Δ\_𝒟) (on the counterfactual domain, occurrence takes equal values event by event and the components satisfy c₂ = α·c₁) ∧ closure(Δ\_𝒟) ∧ MC-G ∧ MC3 ∧ MC4-𝒟 ∧ MC5-ε ∧ H-corr; φ\_Ξ is a position map; F models additionally require the (F) branch of Lemma 15-1a.  
The proof closes in five items:  
　(1) Counterfactual states: by MC4-𝒟 (i), Φ(set(o₁(u), σ)) = set(o₂(u), φ\_I σ); the state after the setting belongs to Δ\_𝒟; MC2⁺(Δ\_𝒟) ∧ closure (∧ CQ) gives MC2^step(Δ\_𝒟) via Lemma 15-1a, and induction on beats gives beat-by-beat correspondence of the states of the two intervention runs.  
　(2) Counterfactual events: by (1) and MC2⁺(Δ\_𝒟), in every beat the occurrence of corresponding kinds takes equal values and the components are scaled by α; by closure, neither side has extra events. The actual run is the special case in which σ takes the actual values, i.e., RC.  
　(3) FD: the occurrence or the component of e₁ changes under σ ⇔ that of e₂ changes under φ\_I σ (α ≠ 0); φ\_I is bijective on 𝒟, so FD₁(e₁) = FD₂(e₂), with the witnesses mapped to each other. With injectivity only, one obtains only FD₁ = 1 ⇒ FD₂ = 1.  
　(4) Paths: MC-G gives a time-preserving isomorphism of the relevant slot hypergraphs; by (2), the actual event graphs are isomorphic; hence Path\_act and Path\_slot take equal values in both directions. With a forward map only, one obtains only the direction of positive values.  
　(5) Act: by (2), \|c₂\| = α\|c₁\|, and by MC5-ε, a₁ = a₂.  
Conclusion (1) (mechanism readings): by (3), (4), (5), FD, Path\_act, Path\_slot and a of corresponding events take equal values on the two sides (with injectivity only, only in the direction of positive values). ∎  
Conclusion (2) (certified three-valued readings):  
　(A) Transfer of positive certificates (ND not needed): the verdict η₁(e₁) = 1 consists of an FD witness and an actual path and consumes no negative-determination declaration; by Conclusion (1), η₂(e₂) = 1; together with the preservation of a and MC1-W, HDyn₁ = 1 ⇒ HDyn₂ = 1 (likewise for κ and Part, with MC1-P added; for in-boundary self-feedback, with the B attribution of the nodes of MC3 added). Only Pres⁺ is issued.  
　(B) Three-valued preservation (ND needed): if ND holds as well, the negative determinations of the two sides are evaluated under the same declarations by the same tier rules and correspond tier by tier by Conclusion (1)—1 ↔ 1, 0(a) ↔ 0(a), 0(b) ↔ 0(b) (both carrying B\_FD), ⊥\_Over ↔ ⊥\_Over; if, further, the adjudication programs of both sides have fully adjudicated the declared domain (no ⊥\_Cov), the three-valued readings of η and κ are preserved, and by strong Kleene composition so are the three-valued readings of HDyn and Part. ∎ Without ND, three-valued Pres is not issued: identical models and the same history S\_AB, with B\_FD declared on one side and not on the other, give HDyn 0 against ⊥ (Appendix B.7, Part 2); with the same declarations on both sides they give 0 against 0 and ⊥ against ⊥, and the positive-certificate example S\_A gives 1 against 1 under both declarations. The undeclared side is recorded as ⊥ and is not changed to 0.  
Aggregation type: in the many-to-one case, (3) issues Pres⁺(FD) only when “exactly one preimage changes with the setting”; the general case is unproved (U15-13).  
Positive controls (the same negative-determination declarations on both sides): Family E against Family E\_α (α = 2, with a, b, ε all multiplied by α) satisfies MC2⁺, and Act, HistCond and HDyn agree event by event (Appendix B.6, Part 2); under scale conjugacy on 𝒦\_g (w, b, lo, hi, θ, v\_reset, A₊, A₋, the teaching current and ε all multiplied by α = 2), Instance P\_α and P have the same q, r, Part, HDyn and the same η for every event (Appendix B.3, Part 9). \[proved (position maps); partial (aggregation)\]  
10.4 Theorem 15-4 (Witnesses for the Necessity of the Antecedents)  
(A) MC2⁺ cannot be replaced by state conjugacy and graph isomorphism: a single position x = 1, two in-boundary processes that always occur and read their own position, source components (+x/10, −x/10), target components (+x/5, −x/5), Φ the identity, ε = 3/20—the net increment is 0 on both sides, MC2^step holds on the whole domain and the slot graphs are isomorphic, yet source Act = 0 and target Act = 1, HDyn 0 against 1 (Appendix B.6, Part 2).  
(B) MC2-r / MC-G are not given by numerical conjugacy: Family E (γ = 0) against the same model with one segment missing from the route chain of hebb—laws, occurrence, components and trajectories agree beat by beat, yet HDyn = 1 and 0 (Appendix B.5, Part 2); a variant in which hebb is replaced by a beat-wise constant law (c taking the actual values) satisfies RC yet gives HDyn = 1 and 0 (Appendix B.3, Part 7 (A); of the type of Theorem 1 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*, inherited).  
(C) MC4-𝒟: for S\_A (the s1 formation history, rest for 8 beats, W = (A, rest, rest)), the intervention domain is changed to the upward-only domain \[actual value, hi\], with laws, run, reads and routes all the same; pinning the positions high from every available beat onward and rerunning up to beat 28 leaves the gates identical to the actual ones beat by beat, so by Lemma C of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* no setting in the upward-only domain changes the gates, and η = 0; under the original domain \[b, hi\], HDyn = 1 (Appendix B.3, Part 7 (B)).  
(D) MC5-ε through availability: a single position z = 1/200, already formed, c = k·z (k ≠ 0): with ε = 1/100 the position is unavailable and η = 0; with ε = 1/1000, η = 1 (directly from D17 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*).  
Hence 𝒞\_run ⊋ 𝒞\_cf strictly (P15-4). \[exact witnesses\]

### 10.5 Theorem 15-5 (Part)

(1) MC1-P ∧ the antecedents of Theorem 15-3 restricted to read-out-class kinds ⇒ Pres(κ), and hence Pres(Part).  
(2) Target Part from transfer of the source HDyn: if the antecedents of Pres⁺(HDyn) hold, the source HDyn₁ = 1, and in 𝔐₂ the carrier of the witness event of HDyn lies in B with Supp\_act ⊆ Supp\_cond (always true for linear laws), then Part₂ = 1. Proof: HDyn₂ = 1, and its witness is conditioned on the same available position (the argument of Lemma 1 (iii) of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*). ∎ This is not preservation of Part: the source Part₁ = 1 does not imply HDyn₁ = 1.  
(3) Scope and counterexample: the frozen control—source Part₁ = 1 (borne by SYN), HDyn₁ = 0; target Part₂ = HDyn₂ = 0 (Appendix B.5, Part 3). This example satisfies neither the antecedents of (2) nor MC1-P of (1) (SYN has no image). On linear own-position family sources, too, Part₁ does not imply HDyn₁ (when \|c\| ≤ ε, Supp\_cond contains the read position), so no subclass “Part is necessarily borne by HDyn” is set up.  
(4) Main pair: Instance P has HDyn₁ = 1, and (2) gives Part₂ = 1 (target-side determined); under the frozen map Part is not preserved, and the failure lies at MC1-P (the Family E encoding contains no read-out process; repairing this requires a declared augmentation by a transmission process, U15-3). \[proved; exact witnesses\]  
10.6 Theorem 15-6 (q, Ident, HistEv; Near-Definitional)  
Under position maps: RC at window end ∧ MC5-ε ∧ MC1-F ⇒ Pres(q). Ident and HistEv are given by the binding of Structural Positions and the correspondence of formation records. Not counted as an increment. Aggregation type: q and availability are defined position by position, and correspondence of path means does not give the atoms position by position—in the positive control for splitting there are 26 groups of final states in which one of the two sub-synapses within a path is ≤ ε while the path mean is \> ε (Appendix B.5, Part 1); an additional condition of within-path agreement is needed. On the beats used, s1 of the main pair is uniform within each path and is not subject to this restriction. \[proved\]  
10.7 Theorem 15-7 (r: Same Route and Cross-Route)  
(1) Same route: MC1-F ∧ RC on the formation beats ∧ MC5-ε, with r evaluated on both sides by the same certification route (Clause 0 against Clause 0, or criterion PS against criterion PS with MC3ˢ and commutation of removal added) ⇒ Pres(r).  
(2) Cross-route (main pair): MC3ˢ gives Pres⁺ from 1 to 2; the target r depends only on whether the image history contains pair, and is target-side determined. From 2 to 1, r is not preserved on the given common image: changing only the threshold of output 0 to θ₀ = 3 (the τ-image is the same as for Instance P), there are 0 effective writes acting on T, and q = r = HDyn = 0, while for the image Src = Ret = HDyn = 1 (Appendix B.3, Part 1). On pure classes with fixed parameters and fixed n, the formation letter always makes output 0 fire (gate argument ≥ 2b + 1 ≥ θ). \[proved; exact witness\]  
10.8 Theorem 15-8 (Fibre Ambiguity on the Common Image)  
Take the frozen map Φ\_main (3.2, including τ) and the declared class 𝒞₁^all (s1 parameters; letters drawn from the formation letter, A, AB, B, rest and arbitrary teaching currents).  
(1) The three declarations S\_A, S\_AB, S\_I (the s1 formation history, rest for 8 beats, window (X, rest, rest) with X being A, AB and A + teaching (1/2, 0) in turn) have the same fibre protocol data (τ-images identical beat by beat, entry states identical), and the T positions, the eligibility traces of the mapped entries and the postsynaptic trace of output 0 agree beat by beat over beats 0–29; HDyn(S\_A) = 1, HDyn(S\_AB) = HDyn(S\_I) = 0 (tier (b), carrying B\_FD); for the common image (Family E), HDyn = 1 (Appendix B.3, Part 1). Hence this fibre is ambiguous for HDyn: by P15-6, it has no recovery rule, and any condition set that certifies positive preservation from 2 to 1 and depends only on fibre protocol data must exclude this fibre. The fibre containing Instance P is likewise ambiguous: the member obtained by changing the three A beats within the window to AB has the same τ-image but HDyn = 0 (Appendix B.5, Part 5).  
(2) Source of the ambiguity (proof): in S\_AB at beat 26, entries 2 and 3 contribute 1/2 through the non-T positions w20, w30, and in S\_I the teaching current contributes 1/2; for every setting in 𝒟₁ (σ ≥ b) the gate argument is ≥ 2b + 1/2 = θ, so the gate is always open, and PLUS (which reads only the eligibility trace) and MINUS (−A₋(d·y₀ + g₀)) are unchanged; for settings at earlier beats, on formation beats the gate argument is ≥ 2b + 1 ≥ θ and the gate is always open, while on rest beats there is no entry and v = 0 after reset does not cross the threshold, so no setting changes any gate on \[u, 28\], and FD = 0 is fully adjudicated. In S\_A, setting the available T positions back to baseline gives 1/2 \< θ, so the gate closes and PLUS disappears, and η = 1. Hence the ambiguity comes from the summands of the gate argument that do not enter the fibre protocol data (non-T entries, out-of-boundary injection) and from the threshold.  
(3) GS is a sufficient condition: GS(t, j) holds ⇒ η₁(PLUS\_ij@t) = 1. Proof: setting back to baseline is a valid same-beat setting in 𝒟₁; g\_j = 0, PLUS\_ij does not occur, and FD = 1; the read edge (w\_ij, t) → PLUS\_ij@t lies on the actual event graph, so Path\_act = 1; read back by Lemma 3.2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*. ∎ In a parameter grid of 216 cases, GS holds in 41, all of which agree (Appendix B.3, Part 2). S\_I is an endpoint example: after setting back to baseline the gate argument is exactly θ, and GS does not hold (Appendix B.5, Part 6).  
Scope: only the ambiguity of the fibres containing the given common image and the image of Instance P is issued; it is not claimed that no valid map or condition set can give transfer from 2 to 1; GS is not the only route. For transferable fibres see Theorem 15-15. \[proved; exact witnesses\]  
10.9 Theorem 15-9 (Target-Side Conditions: HistCond and HDyn Kept Apart)  
Under Φ\_main, let 𝔐₂ belong to the linear own-position family (k ≠ 0, γ ≠ 0, route certification derived from the chain as 1).  
(1) HistCond: for an event e₂ within the window acting on z1, z1 available at t(e₂) ⇒ η₂(e₂) = 1 (Lemma 15-0).  
(2) HDyn: Eff₂ (4.5) ⇒ HDyn₂ = 1. On drive beats hebb has \|c\| ≥ b = 1/10 \> ε₂, so “z1 is available on some drive beat within the window” implies Eff₂; when there are only rest beats, Eff₂ ⇔ on some beat γ·z1 \> ε₂ with z1 available.  
(3) Long-rest example: Family E with the original parameters, pair followed by rest × 100, W = \[100, 100\]: z1 is available and HistCond = 1, \|c\| ≈ 0.000928 ≤ ε₂, Act = 0, HDyn = 0; Eff₂ fails. This HDyn = 0 holds only for this window and this T. In the original window of the main image τ(h₁), Eff₂ holds and HDyn₂ = 1 (Appendix B.5, Part 4).  
(4) Reading: on images where Eff₂ holds, HDyn₂ ≡ 1 regardless of the source, and Pres⁺ from 1 to 2 is target-side determined; on images where Eff₂ fails, HDyn₂ can be 0, and transfer falls under the domain partition of Theorem 15-15. \[proved; exact witnesses\]  
10.10 Theorem 15-10 (Conversion of the Mode of Determination: Occurrence Type versus Strength Type)  
(1) Super-beat encoding obstruction. Let G ∈ 𝒦\_g and let the block length be L ≥ 1, with a block map sending \[u, u + L) to one beat of an F model. Suppose the block-level encoding satisfies (E1) depth is the Trace-Response, (E2\_L) one beat per block, with components reading the block-start state, (E3) exclusivity of local settings, and (E4) on the setting domain the block-level update equals the block update of G exactly. If the block update of some T position in G, as a function of the joint block-start setting value σ, has a jump J ∉ εℤ at an interior point, then no such encoding exists; the sum of the PLUS components within a block is a piecewise-constant function of σ and takes only finitely many values.  
Proof: the position update in one F beat is Φ(σ) = σ + Σ pulses + Σ\_m c\_m(σ)·\[\|c\_m(σ)\| \> ε\], each c\_m of degree ≤ 2; Lemma Q of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* uses only the form of one F beat and applies verbatim at the block level: the difference between the left and right limits lies in εℤ, contradicting E4. Finitely many values: within a block, w and v are affine functions of σ beat by beat, and x and y are constants (the same argument as Theorem S-PAR of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization*); the roots of the gating and clipping comparisons cut the interval into finitely many pieces, the gating record is constant on each piece, and the PLUS component depends only on the gating record and the letters. ∎  
Witness: s1 at beat 26, joint setting w00 = w10 = σ, L = 1, 6, 12, 18: on a 281-point grid, Σ PLUS00 within the block takes 2, 2, 3 and 4 values; at the first switching point the jump of the block sum is J/ε ≈ 12.507, 12.507, 12.695, 12.698, none of them an integer; the jump in the block increment of w00 is ≈ 0.062570–0.064484 (L = 1 is the jump of Appendix B.5 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*) (Appendix B.3, Part 3). That the jump condition holds in general has been verified only on s1 (U15-8). \[proved; exact witness\]  
(2) Staircase character of the rate limit. Antecedents: gating unit j of 𝒦\_g receives a constant drive u ≥ 0 every beat under a constant letter (fixed weights, plasticity off, no coupling between outputs), λ ∈ \[0, 1\], v\_reset = 0, initial value v = 0, θ \> 0. Conclusion: the firing period is n(u) = min{n ≥ 1 : u·Σ\_{k\<n} λ^k ≥ θ} (no firing if no such n exists), the number of firings within L beats is ⌊L/n(u)⌋, and the rate r(u) = 1/n(u) is a monotone nondecreasing step function of u; on any interval on which r is not constant, r is not an affine function of u, and on an interval containing a jump J every affine law has uniform error ≥ J/2. Under the same antecedents the same conclusion holds for a sum of finitely many such units with positive weights.  
Proof: after a reset v = 0; while the unit has not fired, the gate argument at the n-th beat is u·Σ\_{k\<n} λ^k; the partial sums are nondecreasing in n, the threshold is first crossed at n(u), and the cycle repeats after the reset; as u increases, n(u) does not increase; an affine function is continuous, and the sum of its distances from r on the two sides of a jump point is ≥ J. ∎ Finite verification: λ ∈ {0, 1/2, 3/4, 1}, u at 400 points in (0, 2\]; the rate takes 2, 8, 13 and 28 values respectively and is monotone nondecreasing; the period formula and beat-by-beat simulation disagree in 0 cases (Appendix B.3, Part 4).  
Corollary: under the antecedents above and the variable correspondence “Family E y ↔ firing rate, Family E w ↔ fixed synaptic weight”, the constitutive antecedent N1 of Family E (y = βwx, an affine law with nonzero slope in w) is neither the long-block firing rate of this 𝒦\_g mechanism nor the firing rate of a finite positive-weight sum of such units; the MC5-τ of the main pair cannot be realized by this coarsening, long-block counting under constant drive. The corollary does not concern time-varying drive, adaptive weights, other readings or other coarsening methods, and does not deny N1 as a phenomenological antecedent of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* (U15-4). \[proved; finite verification\]  
(3) Conditions for conversion at the R layer.  
　(a) Occurrence type → strength type (linear law): Pres⁺ of HistCond holds when z1 is available and is target-side determined; Pres⁺ of HDyn requires Eff₂ and is target-side determined on the images where Eff₂ holds (Theorem 15-9).  
　(b) Strength type → occurrence type: the fibres in which the given common image and the image of Instance P lie are ambiguous (Theorem 15-8); on the pure class 𝒞₁ᵖ(n) with fixed n, a domain partition by fibre protocol data yields conditional preservation, which is a substantive transfer for n ≤ 23, while for n ≥ 24 the positive-determination domain is empty (Theorem 15-15). GS is one proved sufficient condition; it is not claimed to be the only one.  
　(c) Occurrence type ↔ occurrence type: when on the same-beat setting domain the gate argument is affine in the setting with nonnegative coefficients, its min is attained with all positions set to baseline and its max with all set to the upper bound; a ≥ gate can be flipped by a same-beat setting if and only if min vt \< θ ≤ max vt (when min vt = θ the gate is open under every setting, which is not an FD witness). When TP and MC4-𝒟 hold, the gates on the two sides take the same value under every same-beat setting, and FD for the same-beat part takes the same value; event identity follows D15-11; settings at earlier beats require the full antecedents of Theorem 15-3. Proof: c \> 0 gives vt − θ the same sign on both sides. ∎ \[proved (same-beat part)\]  
　(d) Strength type ↔ strength type (linear law): η depends only on availability, while HDyn depends in addition on effective action; strength-type targets with state-dependent support have not been studied (U15-2).  
10.11 Theorem 15-11 (Intervention Commutation)  
(1) MC4 (state layer and process layer) ∧ the antecedents of Theorem 15-3 on Δ\_I ⇒ after the intervention the mechanism readings are preserved and positive certificates satisfy Pres⁺; three-valued readings in addition require ND (position maps; for the aggregation type, with the same limits as at the end of 10.3). Proof: the intervened instance belongs to ℛ, the commuting square gives the entry correspondence, and Theorem 15-3 is then applied. ∎  
(2) Failure classes and witnesses for the main pair (Appendix B.3, Part 6):  
　Cut: bap off (within W) ↦ cut(N→syn). In the SNN, bap off prevents both PLUS and MINUS from occurring (RELAX is absent, since ρ = 0); there are 0 effective events acting on T within the window, and HDyn = 0. In Family E, cut only sets the route certification of hebb to 0; decay runs as usual and bears HistCond, and HDyn = 1. Failure class: “changes the dynamics” versus “changes only the certification”, and the disabled sets do not correspond. With φ\_I changed to cut ∧ frozen decay, HDyn is 0 on both sides, but under cut hebb still runs as b + k·z, so per-event Act and the trajectories do not correspond.  
　Freeze: plastic off ↦ freeze commutes on the kinds acting on T, and HDyn is 0 on both sides; Part is 1 against 0 (Theorem 15-5 (3)).  
　Erasure: w00, w10 ← b ↦ z1 ← 0 commutes at the state layer (φ\_Ξ(0) = 0). After erasure the SNN gate argument is 2b = 1/2 \< θ, there is no effective event acting on T within the window, and HDyn = 0; in Family E the constant term b = 1/10 \> ε₂ of hebb re-forms z1 at beat 26 by an in-boundary write, decay then reads it, and HDyn = 1. Location: the laws at the zero-Trace-Response point are not conjugate (the Family E write has a constant term b that does not read the Trace-Response, while the SNN write must cross the threshold).  
　Setting domain: Theorem 15-4 (C). I\_m: Theorem 15-12.  
(3) Erasure coincides component by component with I\_min of 11.6 of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* (K and m = min must be declared), so the failure of erasure to commute is at the same time a failure of I\_min to transfer. \[proved; exact witness\]  
10.12 Theorem 15-12 (Additional Conditions for the Transfer of Dynamics)  
Antecedents: in addition to the antecedents of Theorem 15-3 (position maps, or aggregations whose whole-step commutation has been checked; the domains of MC2⁺, MC2^step and MC-G contain the intervened states of every I\_c, c ∈ C\_leg):  
　DT1 (correspondence of depth and aggregation): the Depth of every member of K corresponds through one and the same strictly increasing ψ, and the aggregation schemes correspond as declared.  
　DT2 (I\_m ↦ I\_m): φ\_I maps I\_c to I\_{ψ(c)}; the write-back rules on both sides are valid (declared in advance, unique result, equal-depth identity); ψ(C\_leg,1 ∩ \[min, max\]) = C\_leg,2 ∩ \[ψ(min), ψ(max)\]; the freeze lists correspond, and dependent quantities are recomputed by each side's own laws; the physical branch is listed separately.  
　DT3 (correspondence of reading types): the reading types are the same and are defined on corresponding sets of c on both sides, and the opening events or conducting beats correspond through R\_E.  
　DT4 (convention for m): “for every valid m” transfers through ψ as a C\_B set; “the same m” additionally requires that m commute with ψ.  
　DT5 (Pass and sub-segments): the counting object of Pass, the habitual-travel criterion and the division into sub-segments correspond.  
Conclusion: D, B\_m, B\_m^q and F are preserved (Ω\_W and Bias additionally require Brk to correspond). These dynamics readings are mechanism readings (determined by runs and intervention reruns, and taking 0, 1 or the undefined mark); they do not pass through the negative-determination declarations of the duty atoms; if they are used together with the three-valued readings of the duty atoms, the latter require ND. Proof: D by DT1; B by the correspondence on Δ\_I given by the commuting square and the run correspondence, DT3 and Lemma M1 of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization*; F by DT1, DT5 and B^q on each sub-segment. ∎  
Check on the main pair and necessity (Appendix B.4, B.3 Part 9): the aggregation scheme is load-bearing (with path 0 = (0, 1/2) and path 1 = (1/4, 1/4), mean aggregation gives D = 0 and bottleneck aggregation D = 1); D has the same value on both sides but there is no run correspondence, so it is not certified; the first-opened reading has no object in Family E (no outflow threshold), so B^first does not transfer; the share reading is undefined at small c\* in the SNN but always defined in Family E (with c\* = 0 the Family E share is still (1/2, 1/2)), so the domains of definition do not correspond; a non-homogeneous valid m: m(d) = min + (max − min)²/(1 + max − min), and for d = (0, 1/2), m(2d) = 1/2 ≠ 2·m(d) = 1/3; in the variant with a single formation, path 0 of the SNN opens once within the window and the Family E image three times, so with N = 3 the Familiar Route is 0 against 1 and F does not transfer; at the formal layer I\_m is well-defined on both sides, while at the physical layer it is not preserved (in the SNN it is a state rewriting of the program host; Family E needs the R-pool resource condition and its setting route is undeclared; Family A must exchange structural material with the outside of B\_phys); reading Theorem R-B of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* as the transfer of B\_m and F requires counterfactual conjugacy on Δ\_I (inherited). \[proved\]  
10.13 Theorem 15-13 (The Boundary Pair and the Physical Layer)  
(1) Φ\_enc is a position map (renaming, α = 1) and satisfies MC1–MC5, MC2-r, MC-G (the route chains and slot hypergraphs of the two sides are isomorphic under the renaming) and H-corr; by Theorems 15-1 and 15-3, all formal atoms are preserved for all histories. Finite verification: all histories of length 1–4 × all windows × T ∈ {{z1}, {z2}, {z1, z2}} × (no intervention / route cut throughout), 6012 groups in total; atoms and trajectories agree item by item, and 2196 of the groups have Q = 1 (Appendix B.2, Part 5). This is a corollary of Proposition 5 of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* and is not counted as an increment.  
(2) The physical layer is not preserved: for the Family E history (pair, drive), initial receptor pools of 40 and 10 have the same encoding and both give Q = 1, yet one passes the physical check and the other fails it; I\_m on Family A must exchange structural material with the outside of B\_phys, and Family E under R-pool is resource-feasible but its setting route is undeclared (Appendix B.2, Part 5). The correspondence conditions of this system contain no balance laws, resources or budgets, and the preservation theorems do not cross the physical layer (U15-3). \[proved; finite verification\]  
10.14 Theorem 15-14 (A Definitional Corollary, Not Counted as an Increment)  
If every conjunct of Trace, Part and HDyn is preserved, then Q is preserved. Proof: Q is a strong Kleene conjunction. ∎  
10.15 Theorem 15-15 (Preservation Domains on Pure Classes with a Fixed Formation Count, and the Range of Substantive Transfer)  
Family of declared classes 𝒞₁ᵖ(n) (n ≥ 1 fixed in advance): s1 parameters; the only letters are the formation letter (A, (1, 0)), (A, 0) and rest; the declared history consists of n formations (at beats 0, 3, …, 3n − 3, each followed by two rest beats), k rest beats (k ≥ 0) and a window W = \[t₀, t₀ + \|W\| − 1\] with t₀ = 3n + k, the letters in the window being (A, 0) or rest; T₁ = {w00, w10}, ε₁ = 1/100. Fibre protocol data: n, the window W and the τ-image; n is the label of the declared class, which the target does not execute; it is part of the correspondence protocol, of the same kind as the W declared by φ\_t. The map is the frozen Φ\_main, the image is τ(h₁) on Family E, and T₂ = {z1}.  
(1) Purity: in 𝒞₁ᵖ(n), τ(h) = pair · rest^{3n−1+k} · τ(W); the window contains no formation letter, and (A, 0) ↦ drive, rest ↦ rest correspond one to one, beat by beat; given n and t₀ we have k = t₀ − 3n, and the letters in the window are recovered beat by beat from τ(W). Hence (n, W, τ-image) determines the source declaration uniquely, every fibre contains only one declaration, the fibres of all atoms are pure, and the predicates of 4.4 are all functions of the fibre protocol data. ∎ Finite verification: each class n = 1–6 × k ∈ 0–70 × 8 kinds of window; pairwise distinct within each class (Appendix B.6, Part 3).  
(2) Conclusion (the verdicts of both sides follow the same negative-determination declarations, and clipping individuation follows the upstream rule of agreement between the two readings): on Π\_I, HDyn₁ = HDyn₂ = 1; on Π\_II, HDyn₁ = HDyn₂ = 0; hence Pres(HDyn) holds on Π\_I ∪ Π\_II (conditional preservation, for every n). Whether it is substantive depends on n; see (3). On Π\_III, HDyn₁ = 0 and HDyn₂ = 1; on Π\_IV⁰, HDyn₁ = 0 and HDyn₂ = 1 (when z1 is available in the image at that drive beat); preservation fails on these two domains. Π\_IV\* is handled case by case; on Π\_sat nothing is issued (the verdict can be 1 or ⊥\_Ind).  
(3) The range of substantive transfer:  
　(A) n ≤ 23: Π\_I and Π\_II are both nonempty and both values have legitimate witnesses, so Pres(HDyn) on Π\_I ∪ Π\_II is a substantive transfer (D15-8). Basis: for each n = 1–23 a member of Π\_I was found (finite search over k ∈ {0, 1, 2, 4, 8} and window lengths 1–3, Appendix B.7, Part 5), and its source value 1 and image value 1 are given by the proof of (2); Π\_II is nonempty for every n (k = 60, window all rest, γ·z1 ≤ ε₂ in the image), with 0 on both sides; the two-valued witnesses for n = 1 and n = 23 were additionally evaluated by the adjudicator (Appendix B.7, Part 5). This is a conclusion of case-by-case verification for the listed n; nothing about other n is inferred from the finite search.  
　(B) n ≥ 24: Π\_I is empty. Proof (upper-bound invariant): on the formative part and on the A0 beats in the window, entries 0 and 1 and output 0 are active in the same beat, and on rest beats none of them is active, so x₀ = x₁ = y₀ holds beat by beat, and on rest beats the T positions are not acted upon (ρ = 0, no entry, and after the reset v = 0 does not cross the threshold); each formation pairing gives the T positions a net increase A₊x′ − A₋y′ = (A₊ − A₋)x′ = x′/16 \> 0 (at most 2 after clipping), so after formation w₀₀ = w₁₀ =: w(n) is nondecreasing in n; w(24) ≈ 1.954082 and 2 − w(24) \< 1/16, so 2 − w(n) \< 1/16 for n ≥ 24. On any A0 beat in the window the gate argument is ≥ 2w(n) ≥ 1 = θ, the gate is open, and the pre-clip sum of a T position is w + x′/16 \> 2 (x′ ≥ 1), so the upper clipping necessarily takes effect, the value returns to 2 and the invariant is preserved; hence no window containing A0 satisfies NC\_W, while Π\_I requires an A0 beat in the window, so Π\_I is empty. ∎ Finite verification: for n = 25–40 both positions are 2 after formation; for n = 30, the 504 windows with k ∈ {0, 1, 8, 60} and window lengths 1–6 contain 0 members of Π\_I; with n = 30 and k = 60, the all-rest single-beat window belongs to Π\_II, with 0 on both sides (Appendix B.7, Part 5). Hence on the classes with n ≥ 24, Π\_I ∪ Π\_II carries only zero values, only conditional preservation is issued, and it does not constitute a substantive transfer; this is not a negative determination for the class or the host: windows containing A0 belong to Π\_sat or to other domains, their verdicts are evaluated by the upstream rules, and ⊥ is not read as 0.  
Proof (of (2)): Π\_I — by Theorem 15-8 (3), η₁(PLUS\_i0@t) = 1; the PLUS component A₊x′ᵢ ≥ A₊ = 1/8 \> ε₁, so a = 1, and under the separate reading HDyn = 1; by NC\_W there is no saturated T-position–beat pair in the window, so the joint reading and the separate reading have the same bearing items (the rule of agreement of the two readings in Theorem D2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*), and HDyn₁ = 1; this positive determination draws on no negative-determination declaration; in the image t is a drive beat, hebb has \|c\| ≥ b \> ε₂ and z1 is available, so by Theorem 15-9 (2) HDyn₂ = 1. Π\_II — source: in the window there is no entry and no teaching current, MINUS does not occur, the gate argument = λv₀ \< θ, PLUS does not occur, ρ = 0 so there is no RELAX, there is no clipping, and no event acts on T within the window, so HDyn₁ = 0 (without drawing on B\_FD); the image has no drive, every decay has \|c\| ≤ ε₂, and HDyn₂ = 0. Π\_III — the source gives 0 as for Π\_II; in the image Eff₂ holds, giving 1. Π\_IV⁰ — source: PLUS does not occur in the window (no upper clipping: S ≤ w ≤ hi); after formation w ≥ b and the components at every beat in the window are ≥ −1/128, so there is no lower clipping; the MINUS component has \|c\| = A₋·d·y₀(t); the last formation firing is at beat 3n − 3, the window starts at a beat ≥ 3n and there is no firing after that, so y₀ ≤ 2·(1/2)³ = 1/4 and \|c\| ≤ (1/16)(1/2)(1/4) = 1/128 \< ε₁, a = 0, HDyn₁ = 0 (without drawing on B\_FD); in the image, at a drive beat where z1 is available, Theorem 15-9 (2) gives 1. ∎  
Finite verification (Appendix B.6, Part 3; n ∈ {1, 3, 6}, k ∈ 0–20 ∪ {30, 45, 60}, \|W\| = 3, all 8 kinds of window, 576 declarations in total, including compensating k): Π\_I 264, targets all 1; Π\_II 5, targets all 0; Π\_III 67 and Π\_IV⁰ 168, targets all 1 (sources proved to be 0; not preserved); Π\_IV\* 72, all evaluated by the adjudicator as (1, 1) (a finite result, not issued at family level); spot checks by the adjudicator on the proved domains, 67 (Π\_I 46, Π\_II 5, Π\_III 8, Π\_IV⁰ 8), with 0 disagreements with the proved source values. The same conclusions hold on another 72 declarations with n ∈ {1, 3, 6}, k ∈ {0, 8, 60} (Appendix B.5, Part 5). Among the 576 declarations listed above there are 0 places in total where a T position is clipped within the window, and NC\_W holds throughout (Appendix B.7, Part 1).  
Clipping controls (Appendix B.7, Part 1; only explicit same-beat positive certificates are replayed and the upstream three-valued composition is called; where no certificate has been supplied only ⊥\_Cov is given, not 0): entry already saturated — n = 30, k = 0, W = \[90, 90\], only (A, 0) in the window, w00 = w10 = 2 before the window, GS holds and HDyn₂ = 1 in the image, but both positions are clipped within the window and the joint net increment is 0; the separate reading gives 1 and the joint reading ⊥, and the verdict is HDyn₁ = ⊥\_Ind; first hit of the cap within the window — n = 24, W = \[72, 72\], both positions about 1.954 before the window, both clipped within the window, joint net increment about 0.0459, both readings give 1, and the verdict is HDyn₁ = 1. Both examples belong to Π\_sat and are not in the domain issued as HDyn₁ = 1; under the single-beat window (A, 0), clipping of T positions within the window first appears at n = 24 formations. ⊥ is not read as 0.  
Ambiguity retained for variable n: when n is not fixed, the same image can come from different n — n = 1, k = 15 and n = 6, k = 0, with W = \[18, 18\] containing only (A, 0): the τ-images of the two histories are both pair·rest^17·drive; at the source GS is false and true and HDyn₁ is 0 and 1, while the targets are both 1; among the 1008 declarations n ∈ 1–6 × k ∈ 0–20 × 8 kinds of window, 240 groups with the same (τ-image, window) contain different n (Appendix B.6, Part 3). In the family of classes with fixed n, the two histories belong to 𝒞₁ᵖ(1) (Π\_IV⁰) and 𝒞₁ᵖ(6) (Π\_I) respectively. When n is not fixed, GS measured separately on the source side can serve only as supplementary evidence, not as a transfer given by fibre protocol data.  
Reading: on each class with a fixed n ≤ 23 there is a domain of substantive transfer, Π\_I ∪ Π\_II, characterized by fibre protocol data (Π\_I includes NC\_W); on the classes with n ≥ 24, Π\_I is empty and there is only conditional preservation with zero values; the domains of non-preservation have definite mechanism locations — Π\_III lies in the negative component (Family E decay bears HDyn in rest windows, while in s1, ρ = 0 and there is no corresponding process), and Π\_IV⁰ in the constant term of the write (the Family E drive write has a constant term b that does not read the Trace-Response, while the s1 write must cross the threshold). Instance P belongs to no 𝒞₁ᵖ(n) (its window contains B beats); for P only equal values are recorded. Scope: only the listed family of classes and the listed map; family-level characterizations of Π\_IV\* and Π\_sat are unproved (U15-14). \[proved; finite verification\]  
10.16 Summary of the Checks on the Main Pair (Appendix B.2)  
Law-level checks on the main pair (α = 1): hold — MC1-W, MC4 (plastic off ↦ freeze); fail — MC1-F, MC1-P, MC2-r, MC2-m, the law type of MC2⁺, MC3 (MINUS ↦ decay), MC4 (bap off ↦ cut), MC5-ε, MC5-τ. With α = 1/10, MC5-ε holds and the rest are unchanged. Instance P: q = r = Part = HDyn = Q = 1 (M1₀ certification of r 36/36; η = 1 for all 12 events acting on T within the window, consistent with 7.5 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*); on the Family E images (both under τ and under the static letter function) every atom is 1. The two sides have equal values; by Theorems 15-5, 15-7–15-9 and 15-15, on Instance P only the target-side determined Pres⁺ and Part₂ = 1 are issued, and no transfer of HDyn is issued.

Section 11  
Counterexamples and Boundary Witnesses  
All the following failure boundaries lie within the working input of Section 2; for each, the formalization, the witness and the affected atoms are given.  
11.1 Mismatched Mode of Determination (MC2-m Fails)  
Formalization: in R\_E, PLUS ↦ hebb, with Md^law(PLUS) = {occurrence} and Md^law(hebb) = {strength}. Conclusion: at the F layer there is no correspondence within the proved antecedents (Theorem 15-10 (1) (2)); at the R layer, 1→2 is target-side determined on the images where Eff₂ holds (Theorem 15-9), and 2→1 is ambiguous on the fibre containing the given common image (Theorem 15-8); on the pure class with fixed n there is conditional preservation on Π\_I ∪ Π\_II (Π\_I includes the no-clipping condition NC\_W; substantive for n ≤ 23, Π\_I empty for n ≥ 24), no preservation on Π\_III ∪ Π\_IV⁰, and nothing issued on Π\_sat (Theorem 15-15). The forced F encoding “occurrence as actually declared, constant law” reads HDyn\_F = 0 while the R layer reads 1; this is inherited from the end of 7.5 (2) of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* and not redone. Affected atoms: HistCond (PLUS, and MINUS through the gating), and hence HDyn, Q and in-boundary self-feedback.  
11.2 Mismatched Bearing of the Negative Component  
The SNN depression component −A₋(d·y\_j + g\_j) reads the weight in step fashion through same-beat gating (11.5 (3) of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization*). Under MINUS ↦ decay, MC1-W holds (both have the duty class negative component, carrier B and target T), while the law type of MC2⁺ (gated step versus linear own-position), MC3 (synapse–output unit–synapse versus synapse–synapse) and MC4 (bap disables MINUS, cut does not reach decay) fail (Appendix B.2, Part 2). Witnesses: under bap off ↦ cut, HDyn is 0 against 1 (Appendix B.3, Part 6); in S\_AB, η(MINUS) = 0(b) while η(decay) = 1 in the image (Appendix B.3, Part 1); in Instance N, MINUS@5 depends on T while \|c\| = 3/512 \< ε₁ (Appendix B.3, Part 5). The non-preservation on Π\_III also falls here. Affected atoms: HDyn after intervention; per-event Act and HistCond.  
11.3 Nonlinear Mechanisms without Correspondence  
Threshold and reset: Theorem 15-10 (1) (2). Clipping: CLIP has no image in the Family E encoding (Family E replaces clipping with a domain of validity), R\_E is undefined on CLIP, and preservation under clipping saturation is undetermined (U15-5). Cancellation of potentiation and depression: Proposition DG-1 of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* (with A₊ = A₋ and strict same-beat timing, w is constant) — under Φ\_main such SNN instances leave no Trace-Response during the formation period, while pair in the image leaves a trace as usual, so Trace is not preserved. Affected atoms: HistCond and HDyn (threshold, reset); Trace (cancellation).  
11.4 Isomorphic Encoding without Preservation at the Physical Layer  
Theorem 15-13 (2). What is affected is the physical construction certificate of C-A of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* and the physical feasibility of I\_m, not the atoms of S₁₅; the preservation theorems hold only at the formal layer.  
11.5 Preserving Only State Trajectories or Actual Runs  
Theorem 1 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* (π the same while Act, HistCond and Q differ); the stronger form in this system: actual-run correspondence, state-transition conjugacy and graph isomorphism still do not suffice to preserve HistCond or HDyn (Theorem 15-4 (A) (B) (C)).  
11.6 Interventions That Do Not Commute (MC4 Fails)  
Theorem 15-11 (2): cut (changing the dynamics versus changing only the certification), freeze (commutes, but Part is not preserved), erasure (commutes at the state layer, but the laws at the zero-Trace-Response point are not conjugate), setting domain (Theorem 15-4 (C)).

### 11.7 Fibre Ambiguity

Gating reads that do not enter the fibre protocol data (non-T entries, out-of-boundary injection) and parameters (the threshold θ) make the source atom take two values on the same image: Theorems 15-8 and 15-7 (2); only the given fibres are issued.  
11.8 Reading Type and Domain of Definition; the Counting Object of Pass  
The DT3 and DT5 witnesses of Theorem 15-12.  
11.9 Encoding That Omits a Read-Out Process  
Theorem 15-5 (3).

### 11.10 ε Not Corresponding under Scaling

Theorems 15-2 and 15-4 (D).  
11.11 No Correspondence for the Negative Component or the Constant Term of the Write  
Π\_III and Π\_IV⁰ of Theorem 15-15.  
11.12 Nonlinear Aggregation Does Not Inherit Event Induction; Mixed Thresholds Do Not Commute at the F Layer  
The algebraic example and the mixed-threshold example of Lemma 15-1a.  
11.13 Fibre Ambiguity under a Variable Formation Count  
The final paragraph of Theorem 15-15: when n is not fixed, the same image comes from n = 1 and from n = 6, with source HDyn 0 and 1.

### 11.14 Clipping Individuation

Declarations that satisfy GS with the image available but have T positions clipped within the window (Π\_sat) can have the verdict ⊥\_Ind (entry-already-saturated example) or 1 (example of the first hit of the cap within the window); positive determination is restricted to NC\_W (Theorem 15-15).  
11.15 Mismatched Negative-Determination Declarations  
With identical models but different B\_FD declarations on the two sides, HDyn is 0 against ⊥ (Theorem 15-3 (2)); three-valued preservation requires ND.

Section 12  
Breach  
This system is breached if any one of the following holds.  
K15-1  Internal contradiction: some mapping class is declared to preserve HDyn while its validity conditions allow an acting event to be mapped to a non-acting event.  
K15-2  Read-back conflict: there is a valid pair satisfying all antecedents of some theorem on which an atom claimed to be preserved differs between the two sides; isomorphic encoding is read back as physical feasibility; sameness of mechanism, source or carrier is read back from identical state equations or identical actual runs.  
K15-3  Omission of a load-bearing symbol from the correspondence table: carrier attribution, mode of determination, temporal order, reading type, aggregation scheme, effective-booking truncation or the process-layer effect of an intervention is omitted in a way that affects the value of HDyn, of in-boundary self-feedback or of B.  
K15-4  Mismatch of relation properties: the claims of P15-1–P15-6 disagree with the actual properties.  
K15-5  An antecedent of a preservation theorem contains equality of atom values or qualification values and is counted as an increment.  
K15-6  Quantifier overreach: writing the ambiguity of one fibre as an impossibility for all condition sets or all maps; calling a condition set minimal when it has not been shown irredundant by deleting items; writing a finite verification as a family-level theorem (as for Π\_IV\*); issuing three-valued preservation without ND; calling the verdict of a single individuation reading the common upstream verdict.  
K15-7  Purity overreach: asserting that fibres are pure on a class whose protocol parameters are not fixed and whose injectivity is not proved, and issuing transfers on that basis.  
The following do not constitute a breach: correspondence conditions on real hosts not verified; the read-back antecedents of some instance not closed; SNN verdicts belonging to the algorithmic level and to model mode; a correspondence that is undefined on an instance being recorded as undefined (such as CLIP having no image); no measurements on real hosts; equal values on both sides that this system does not certify; target-side determined transfers; ambiguous fibres recorded as non-transferring; nothing issued outside the pure classes; Π\_IV\* not characterized at family level; nothing issued for nonlinear aggregations whose whole-step commutation has not been checked; non-preservation at the physical layer; the rate-staircase corollary not denying N1 of Family E as a phenomenological antecedent; full formal preservation on the boundary pair (a corollary of Proposition 5 of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*); citing conclusions of *The Necessity Form of Field-Concretization Qualification* as gating-pivot conditions.

Section 13  
Conditional Items and Status

### 13.1 Load-Bearing Conditions

The lemmas and theorems of Section 10 hold under the listed antecedents, all of which are written into the hypotheses of the respective items: the whole-step commutation and event summation rules (Theorem 15-1, Lemma 15-1a) and CQ for F models; event-wise conjugacy and closure, MC-G, MC4-𝒟 and MC5-ε (Theorem 15-3), with three-valued readings additionally requiring the correspondence ND of negative-determination declarations (Theorem 15-3 (2)); source HDyn = 1 (Theorem 15-5 (2)); Eff₂ (Theorem 15-9 (2)); constant drive, fixed weights, zero reset and zero initial value (Theorem 15-10 (2)); the jump condition (Theorem 15-10 (1)); fixed n, fibre protocol data and the no-clipping condition NC\_W for positive determination (Theorem 15-15); DT1–DT5 (Theorem 15-12). There are no load-bearing H entries. Reading the duties back onto the hosts requires the antecedents of Section 8.

### 13.2 Three Kinds of Strength

Model conclusions (A): all lemmas and theorems of Section 10 and the relation properties of Section 7, and all witnesses of Section 11; limited to the declared model instances, mapping classes and contracts; the SNN conclusions belong to the algorithmic level of a program host; the conclusions on Families A and E hold only within the idealizations and constitutive antecedents of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*; on Instance P only equal values are recorded.  
Conditional inference for real hosts (B): any transfer statement with a real host at either end must, as required at Level 3, list the unverified correspondence conditions and read-back antecedents. Candidate: the intervention correspondence between an *E. coli* strain with a fixed modification state and frozen plasticity in the model — by Theorem 15-11, freezing commutes on the kinds acting on T, while the bearing of Part requires the read-out process to have an image; the mechanism model of this strain has not been frozen, and none of the listed correspondence conditions has been verified. This system issues no class-B statement.  
Empirical certification (C): none. The three columns do not substitute for one another; equal values or successful transfers between models are not written up as certification on a real host; the lack of an existing experiment is not grounds for a verdict of 0, for deleting the object or for stopping the research.

### 13.3 Open Items

　U15-1  Substantive transfer from occurrence type to occurrence type: TP proves only the same-beat part; a complete construction taking Family 1ᵍ of 6.7 of *The Necessity Form of Field-Concretization Qualification* or a thresholded variant of Family E as the target, and the propagation of settings at earlier beats.  
　U15-2  Transfer conditions on strength-type targets with state-dependent support (bilinear-partner support).  
　U15-3  Transfer conditions at the physical layer (correspondence of balance laws, resources and budgets); declared augmentation of the transmission process of Family E.  
　U15-4  A coarsening that realizes N1 of Family E (stochastic gating, continuous time, continuous populations) — outside 𝒦\_g.  
　U15-5  Correspondence of clipping (the separate and joint readings) under Φ; preservation at saturated position–beat pairs.  
　U15-6  The boundary case of Lemma 15-0 (the acting position available at an earlier beat but not at t(e)).  
　U15-7  A witness for the 2→1 direction of cross-route r that changes no parameter and changes only unmapped auxiliary states.  
　U15-8  General conditions on 𝒦\_g under which the jump condition J ∉ εℤ of the super-beat encoding obstruction holds.  
　U15-9  Failure typing on the parameter grid of Instance P (s1 parameters) and its class-by-class correspondence with 11.3.  
　U15-10  The timing and position modes of determination: to be studied in models in which timing or position is determined by the Trace-Response.  
　U15-11  The formal predicate of in-boundary self-feedback still depends on the unanswered question of 13.2 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* (the in-boundary attribution of the nodes of evidence paths).  
　U15-12  A general verification procedure for MC2^step(Δ\_𝒟) and MC-G.  
　U15-13  Preservation of HistCond under the aggregation type (many-to-one).  
　U15-14  Family-level characterization of Π\_IV\* in the pure class 𝒞₁ᵖ(n); domain partitions on larger pure classes.  
　U15-15  Repairing Π\_III: preservation conditions on rest windows when ρ \> 0 is taken on the SNN side (RELAX ↔ decay).  
　U15-16  Another protocol for variable n (a subsequent-formation marker output independently by the transducer, or including the boundary of the formative part in the target protocol data), and how the target executes such letters.  
　U15-17  Necessary and sufficient conditions for Q to commute with weighted sums (CQ is only a sufficient condition).

Section 14  
Interface

### 14.1 Inputs

*The General Form of Field Concretization* v1.0: the formal master formula, the bearing relation, organizational conditioning, action, the five modes of determination of HistCond, the process-identification rule, the convergence-round lemma, the in-boundary self-feedback type of Section 7.  
*The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* v1.0: D1–D22, 4.2, Section 7, 8.2, Theorems 1, 2, 3 and 5, 13.2.  
*The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0: Definitions 1–7, Proposition 1, Lemmas R1–R3, 3.3, 3.6, 4.2, 4.3, Lemma 3.2, Lemma C, Theorem B2, 7.5; the appendix programs.  
*The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0: C-C, 4.2, 4.3, 5.2, 5.3, Lemma 1, Proposition 4, Proposition 5 and the four-layer separation, 7.2.  
*The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics* v1.2: the definitions of D, B\_m, B\_m^q and F and the proved relations among them (cited through *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0).  
*The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0: 2.4, D4, D7–D10, D14–D17, 4.1, Lemma M1, Theorems C-I, W-2, S-PAR, J-1, W-1 and R-B, Proposition DG-1, 11.2, 11.5, 11.6; the appendix programs fam\_jia\_wu and common12.  
*The Adjudication Form of Material Field Concretization* v1.0: 5.8.  
*The Necessity Form of Field-Concretization Qualification* v1.0: Theorem 14-1⁺, C\_LS⁺, Theorem 14-7, Lemma 14-L and the structural observation on the linear own-position family.

### 14.2 Outputs

To *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*: a checklist of the correspondence conditions for citing instance certificates across instances (Level 1 conditions of 8.2, including MC2-r, MC-G, MC2⁺ and the fibres issued); transfer of occurrence-type HistCond from strength-type models can be issued on pure fibres with a domain partition of the Theorem 15-15 type (positive determination restricted to the decidable subdomain without clipping; substantive transfer only for classes with n ≤ 23), is not issued on ambiguous fibres, and no transfer conditions are given for other routes; transfer of certified three-valued readings requires the correspondence of negative-determination declarations; GS as a sufficient condition for same-beat positive determination.  
To *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization*: the additional conditions DT1–DT5 for the transfer of dynamics and the check on the main pair; the s1 bifurcation reading and Family E have no common object in the first-opened reading, and their domains of definition do not correspond in the share reading; the failure of transfer when erasure is I\_min.  
To *The Adjudication Form of Material Field Concretization*: record composition for the same object, the same T and the same process inventory belongs to 5.8 of that paper; correspondences between different law kinds, read sets, objects or T belong to this system.  
To *The Necessity Form of Field-Concretization Qualification*: C\_LS⁺ and Theorem 14-1⁺ are family-level instances of GS-type conditions; in the linear own-position family HistCond depends only on availability, while HDyn additionally requires effective action.  
To *The Discrimination Form of Sedimentation Dynamics*: Theorem 15-4 and P15-4 give the formal basis for “mechanisms are not deemed the same because their state equations or actual runs are the same”; this is the dual discipline of “differing from an old model does not automatically mean a new type”, and the two belong to different places.  
To the adjudication of biological neural, spiking-neural-network and riverbed hosts: transfer statements on real hosts must, as required at Level 3, list the unverified correspondence conditions, and are at most class B; the reading types and the counting objects of Pass must correspond; the fibre protocol data must be fixed in advance.  
To *The Master Map of the Field Concretization Group Form*: the formal registration of what is “common” and what is “different” between neural and non-neural hosts follows the condition-set table of 10.17; local correspondences are not written up as universal identity.  
Boundary with the comparative meta-form (the Equivalence Form): the main output of this system consists of theorems of the form “condition set ⇒ atom preservation”, domain partitions and failure boundaries; the registration table of 10.17 and the list of equal values on Instance P are intermediate products, not an adjudication of the equivalence level of two models.  
14.3 Sources of the Load-Bearing Antecedents  
The load-bearing antecedents come only from the listed versions of the papers listed in 14.1; this system uses no conclusions from any other paper.

## Appendix A  Program Source

Runtime environment: Python 3.10 or later, standard library only. The following 16 files are placed in the same directory; A.1–A.6 are, in order, verbatim identical to Appendix A.1, A.2, A.3, A.5, A.4 and A.6 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0, and A.7 and A.8 are, in order, verbatim identical to Appendix A.4 and A.6 of *The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization* v1.0; their SHA-256 is checked by A.9 at load time; A.11–A.16 are the run entries. The programs give finite verification and exact witnesses; universal conclusions rest on the proofs in the main text.  
A.1  snn\_core.py  Program of the frozen SNN Instance P (s1 parameters) (verbatim identical to Appendix A.1 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0)

~~~python
"""Frozen SNN instance s1: a replayable SNN instance (program host). Exact rational arithmetic, Python standard library, deterministic, replayable event by event.
Host instance χ_SNN = (P, L, B, W):
  P this program; L the algorithmic level (discrete time steps); B = {4 input neurons, 2 output LIF neurons, 8 synapses}; W the declared window.
  Input-neuron firing is fixed by an external stimulus sequence (out-of-boundary signal); the teaching current teach is injected into output 0 only during the formation phase (out-of-boundary signal).
  Output neurons: LIF, v ← v·LEAK + Σ w_ij·s_i + I_ext; when v ≥ θ the neuron fires and resets to 0.
  Plasticity: pair-based STDP (trace form): presynaptic traces x_i and postsynaptic traces y_j are multiplied by DEC each step and incremented by 1 on firing;
    when postsynaptic neuron j fires (via the backpropagated signal reaching the synapse), w_ij += A_PLUS·x_i; when presynaptic neuron i fires, w_ij -= A_MINUS·y_j;
    weights are clipped to [0, W_MAX]. Optional relaxation toward baseline (RELAX, default 0).
  Intervention switches: plastic (plasticity on/off); bap (whether postsynaptic firing propagates back to the synapse; when off, STDP does not see postsynaptic firing, i.e. the route is cut).
Trace-Response: above-baseline weight z_ij = w_ij − W0.
"""
from fractions import Fraction as F
import hashlib
N_IN, N_OUT = 4, 2
W0 = F(1, 4)
LEAK, THETA, DEC = F(3, 4), F(1), F(1, 2)
A_PLUS, A_MINUS, W_MAX = F(1, 8), F(1, 16), F(2)
PAT = {'A': (1, 1, 0, 0), 'B': (0, 0, 1, 1), 'AB': (1, 1, 1, 1), 'none': (0, 0, 0, 0)}

def init(w=None):
    return dict(w=[[W0 if w is None else w[i][j] for j in range(N_OUT)] for i in range(N_IN)],
                v=[F(0)] * N_OUT, x=[F(0)] * N_IN, y=[F(0)] * N_OUT)

def step(st, inp, teach=(0, 0), plastic=True, bap=True, relax=F(0)):
    """One step: returns (new state, event record). The event record lists presynaptic firing, postsynaptic firing and the plasticity components of each synapse (positive, negative, relaxation)."""
    w = [row[:] for row in st['w']]
    s_in = PAT[inp]
    v = [st['v'][j] * LEAK + sum(w[i][j] * s_in[i] for i in range(N_IN)) + teach[j] for j in range(N_OUT)]
    s_out = [1 if v[j] >= THETA else 0 for j in range(N_OUT)]
    v = [F(0) if s_out[j] else v[j] for j in range(N_OUT)]
    x = [st['x'][i] * DEC + s_in[i] for i in range(N_IN)]
    y = [st['y'][j] * DEC + s_out[j] for j in range(N_OUT)]
    comp = {}
    for i in range(N_IN):
        for j in range(N_OUT):
            pos = A_PLUS * x[i] if (plastic and bap and s_out[j]) else F(0)
            neg = -A_MINUS * y[j] if (plastic and s_in[i] and bap) else F(0)
            rel = -relax * (w[i][j] - W0) if plastic else F(0)
            nw = min(W_MAX, max(F(0), w[i][j] + pos + neg + rel))
            comp[(i, j)] = (pos, neg, rel, nw - w[i][j])
            w[i][j] = nw
    ev = dict(inp=inp, teach=teach, s_in=s_in, s_out=tuple(s_out), comp=comp)
    return dict(w=w, v=v, x=x, y=y), ev

def run(hist, st=None, **kw):
    """hist: [(input name, teaching current)]. Returns (final state, event log)."""
    st = st or init()
    log = []
    for inp, teach in hist:
        st, ev = step(st, inp, teach, **kw)
        log.append(ev)
    return st, log

def log_hash(log):
    """Fingerprint of the frozen log: SHA-256 of the events serialized in a fixed order."""
    h = hashlib.sha256()
    for ev in log:
        h.update(repr((ev['inp'], ev['teach'], ev['s_in'], ev['s_out'],
                       sorted((k, tuple(str(c) for c in v)) for k, v in ev['comp'].items()))).encode())
    return h.hexdigest()

def z(st):
    return [[st['w'][i][j] - W0 for j in range(N_OUT)] for i in range(N_IN)]
~~~

A.2  gate\_class.py  Implementation of the event-gating class 𝒦\_g (verbatim identical to Appendix A.2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0)

~~~python
"""Independent implementation of the formal definition of the event-gating class 𝒦_g (main text 3.1, Definitions 1–3), and beat-by-beat finite verification that “the frozen SNN is a member of 𝒦_g” (main text, Proposition 1).
Standard library only, exact rational arithmetic. Run: python3 gate_class.py
The frozen snn_core.py (Appendix A.1) is loaded only as the object under check; its SHA-256 is verified before loading.
Parameters of a K_g member G (Definition 1):
  n_in, n_out          number of entry units, number of gating units; Structural Positions X = {(i, j)} (fully connected)
  pat: Σ_in -> {0,1}^n_in  entry letter to entry pulse vector (including rest 'none' -> zero vector)
  lam[j], theta[j], vreset[j]   leak, threshold, reset value
  dx[i], dy[j]         eligibility-trace decay
  Ap, Am, rho          potentiation amplitude, depression amplitude, relaxation rate
  b[i][j], lo[i][j], hi[i][j]   baseline, valid interval
Protocol switches (declared items, not part of the state): plastic, bap
Letter σ = (entry name, I vector); I is the out-of-boundary injection (teaching current).
"""
import sys, hashlib, random, itertools, importlib.util
from fractions import Fraction as F
sys.dont_write_bytecode = True
SNN_DIR = __import__('os').path.dirname(__import__('os').path.abspath(__file__))   # the frozen snn_core.py sits in the same directory as this program (Appendix A.1)
FROZEN_SHA = {'snn_core.py': '4e0fd45d2442890ecf3a7687ea285f89ed161e6fb4ab1083ca0fb79821960e49'}

class Gate:
    """A member of K_g (Definition 1). State o = (w, v, x, y); w[i][j] are the Structural Positions, v, x, y the auxiliary positions."""
    def __init__(self, n_in, n_out, pat, lam, theta, vreset, dx, dy, Ap, Am, rho, b, lo, hi):
        self.n_in, self.n_out, self.pat = n_in, n_out, dict(pat)
        self.lam, self.theta, self.vreset = list(lam), list(theta), list(vreset)
        self.dx, self.dy = list(dx), list(dy)
        self.Ap, self.Am, self.rho = Ap, Am, rho
        self.b, self.lo, self.hi = b, lo, hi
        self.check_domain()
    def check_domain(self):
        """Parameter constraints of Definition 1; anything that violates them is not a K_g member."""
        assert 'none' in self.pat and all(v == 0 for v in self.pat['none'])
        assert all(len(p) == self.n_in and all(c in (0, 1) for c in p) for p in self.pat.values())
        assert all(0 <= l <= 1 for l in self.lam) and all(0 <= d <= 1 for d in self.dx + self.dy)
        assert 0 <= self.rho <= 1 and self.Ap >= 0 and self.Am >= 0
        for i in range(self.n_in):
            for j in range(self.n_out):
                assert self.lo[i][j] <= self.b[i][j] <= self.hi[i][j]
        return True
    # ---------- Definition 2: per-beat execution order (eight stages, reading the pre-beat state) ----------
    def step(self, o, sigma, plastic=True, bap=True):
        """Returns (o', rec). rec lists the gate arguments vt, g, and for each synapse (P+, P-, Pr, pre-clip sum, post-clip value)."""
        w, v, x, y = o
        name, I = sigma
        s = self.pat[name]                                                     # (1) read input
        vt = [self.lam[j] * v[j] + sum(w[i][j] * s[i] for i in range(self.n_in)) + I[j]
              for j in range(self.n_out)]                                      # (2) integrate (pre-beat w)
        g = [1 if vt[j] >= self.theta[j] else 0 for j in range(self.n_out)]    # (3) gate, comparison >=
        v2 = [self.vreset[j] if g[j] else vt[j] for j in range(self.n_out)]    # (4) reset
        x2 = [self.dx[i] * x[i] + s[i] for i in range(self.n_in)]              # (5) eligibility traces
        y2 = [self.dy[j] * y[j] + g[j] for j in range(self.n_out)]
        w2 = [[None] * self.n_out for _ in range(self.n_in)]
        comp = {}
        for i in range(self.n_in):
            for j in range(self.n_out):
                pp = self.Ap * x2[i] if (plastic and bap and g[j]) else F(0)   # (6) potentiation: only if g_j = 1
                pm = -self.Am * y2[j] if (plastic and bap and s[i]) else F(0)  #     depression: only if s_i = 1
                pr = -self.rho * (w[i][j] - self.b[i][j]) if (plastic and self.rho != 0) else F(0)
                S = w[i][j] + pp + pm + pr
                nw = min(self.hi[i][j], max(self.lo[i][j], S))                 # (7) clipping
                w2[i][j] = nw
                comp[(i, j)] = (pp, pm, pr, S, nw)
        rec = dict(s=s, I=tuple(I), vt=vt, g=tuple(g), comp=comp)              # (8) read out g
        return (w2, v2, x2, y2), rec
    def init(self, w=None):
        return ([[self.b[i][j] if w is None else w[i][j] for j in range(self.n_out)] for i in range(self.n_in)],
                [F(0)] * self.n_out, [F(0)] * self.n_in, [F(0)] * self.n_out)
    def run(self, hist, o=None, **kw):
        o = o or self.init()
        traj, recs = [o], []
        for sig in hist:
            o, r = self.step(o, sig, **kw)
            traj.append(o); recs.append(r)
        return traj, recs

def snn_params(S):
    """Reads the K_g parameters from the current global constants of the snn_core module (correspondence between Definition 1 and lines 15–20 of snn_core; main text, Proposition 1)."""
    n_in, n_out = S.N_IN, S.N_OUT
    full = lambda val: [[val] * n_out for _ in range(n_in)]
    return dict(n_in=n_in, n_out=n_out, pat=S.PAT, lam=[S.LEAK] * n_out, theta=[S.THETA] * n_out,
                vreset=[F(0)] * n_out, dx=[S.DEC] * n_in, dy=[S.DEC] * n_out, Ap=S.A_PLUS, Am=S.A_MINUS,
                b=full(S.W0), lo=full(F(0)), hi=full(S.W_MAX))

def load_snn_core():
    p = SNN_DIR + '/snn_core.py'
    h = hashlib.sha256(open(p, 'rb').read()).hexdigest()
    assert h == FROZEN_SHA['snn_core.py'], 'snn_core.py is not the frozen version'
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('snn_core', p)
    m = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(m)
    return m, h

def snn_gate(S, rho=F(0)):
    return Gate(rho=rho, **snn_params(S))

def WIN0():
    return [('A', (0, 0)), ('none', (0, 0)), ('none', (0, 0)), ('B', (0, 0)), ('none', (0, 0)), ('none', (0, 0))] * 3

def compare_run(S, G, hist, w_init=None, o_init=None, plastic=True, bap=True, relax=F(0)):
    """Runs the same history through snn_core.step and through G.step and compares the full state and the plasticity components beat by beat.
    Returns (number of beats, number of clipping events, number of times the gate argument equals the threshold exactly, list of mismatches)."""
    if o_init is not None:
        st = dict(w=[r[:] for r in o_init[0]], v=list(o_init[1]), x=list(o_init[2]), y=list(o_init[3]))
        o = (o_init[0], o_init[1], o_init[2], o_init[3])
    else:
        st = S.init(w_init); o = G.init(w_init)
    bad, nclip, ntie = [], 0, 0
    for t, (inp, teach) in enumerate(hist):
        st, ev = S.step(st, inp, teach, plastic=plastic, bap=bap, relax=relax)
        o, r = G.step(o, (inp, teach), plastic=plastic, bap=bap)
        ntie += sum(1 for j in range(G.n_out) if r['vt'][j] == G.theta[j])
        if st['w'] != o[0] or st['v'] != o[1] or st['x'] != o[2] or st['y'] != o[3]:
            bad.append((t, 'state'))
        if tuple(ev['s_out']) != r['g'] or tuple(ev['s_in']) != tuple(r['s']):
            bad.append((t, 'spikes'))
        for k, (pp, pm, pr, Ssum, nw) in r['comp'].items():
            c = ev['comp'][k]
            w_before = Ssum - pp - pm - pr
            if (c[0], c[1], c[2], c[3]) != (pp, pm, pr, nw - w_before):
                bad.append((t, 'comp', k))
            if nw != Ssum:
                nclip += 1
    return len(hist), nclip, ntie, bad

def main():
    S, h = load_snn_core()
    print('1. Frozen-version verification')
    print('  snn_core.py SHA-256 =', h, '(matches frozen value)')
    base = {k: getattr(S, k) for k in ['LEAK', 'THETA', 'DEC', 'A_PLUS', 'A_MINUS', 'W0', 'W_MAX']}
    def setp(**kw):
        for k, val in base.items():
            setattr(S, k, kw.get(k, val))
    G = snn_gate(S)
    print('  Definition 1 parameter domain: λ =', G.lam[0], ', d =', G.dx[0], ', ρ = 0 (RELAX default), A₊ =', G.Ap, ', A₋ =', G.Am,
          ', b =', G.b[0][0], ', [lo, hi] = [', G.lo[0][0], ',', G.hi[0][0], '], θ =', G.theta[0], ', v_reset =',
          G.vreset[0], '; entry vectors', sorted(G.pat.items()), '— all constraints satisfied:', G.check_domain())
    FORM = [('A', (F(1), 0)), ('none', (0, 0)), ('none', (0, 0))] * 6
    CTRL = [('A', (0, 0)), ('none', (0, 0)), ('none', (0, 0))] * 6
    REST = [('none', (0, 0))] * 8
    WIN = WIN0()
    tot = dict(ticks=0, clip=0, tie=0, bad=[])
    def acc(res, label):
        n, c, ti, bad = res
        tot['ticks'] += n; tot['clip'] += c; tot['tie'] += ti; tot['bad'] += [(label,) + b for b in bad]
        return n, c, ti, len(bad)
    print('2. Frozen declared history (as used by snn_instance.py), compared beat by beat: step of K_g Definition 2 vs snn_core.step')
    rows = []
    rows.append(('FORM (formation phase)', acc(compare_run(S, G, FORM), 'FORM')))
    rows.append(('CTRL (no-teaching control)', acc(compare_run(S, G, CTRL), 'CTRL')))
    rows.append(('FORM+REST+WIN (with Trace-Response)', acc(compare_run(S, G, FORM + REST + WIN), 'FRW')))
    st_r = S.run(REST, st=S.run(FORM)[0])[0]
    o_er = (S.init()['w'], st_r['v'], st_r['x'], st_r['y'])
    rows.append(('erasure-side WIN (R1(a′): weights reset, v, x, y carried over from the end-of-rest state, plasticity on)', acc(compare_run(S, G, WIN, o_init=o_er), 'ERASE')))
    o_r = (st_r['w'], st_r['v'], st_r['x'], st_r['y'])
    rows.append(('route-cut WIN (bap off)', acc(compare_run(S, G, WIN, o_init=o_r, bap=False), 'BAPOFF')))
    rows.append(('frozen-plasticity WIN (plastic off)', acc(compare_run(S, G, WIN, o_init=o_r, plastic=False), 'FROZEN')))
    rows.append(('relaxation RELAX = 1/10, FORM+REST+WIN', acc(compare_run(S, snn_gate(S, F(1, 10)), FORM + REST + WIN, relax=F(1, 10)), 'RELAX')))
    for lab, (n, c, ti, nb) in rows:
        print(f'  {lab}: {n} beats, clipping {c}, gate argument = θ {ti} times, mismatches {nb}')
    print('3. Parameter grid (the 108 groups of snn_scan.py; snn_core global constants rewritten as it does, K_g parameters read out alongside)')
    ng, tie_grid = 0, 0
    for W0, AP, teach, n in itertools.product([F(1, 8), F(1, 4), F(3, 8), F(1, 2)], [F(1, 16), F(1, 8), F(1, 4)],
                                              [F(1, 2), F(1), F(2)], [1, 3, 6]):
        setp(W0=W0, A_PLUS=AP)
        Gp = snn_gate(S)
        form = ([('A', (teach, 0))] + [('none', (0, 0))] * 2) * n
        ctrl = ([('A', (0, 0))] + [('none', (0, 0))] * 2) * n
        tie_grid += acc(compare_run(S, Gp, form + REST + WIN0()), 'GRID')[2]
        tie_grid += acc(compare_run(S, Gp, ctrl), 'GRIDC')[2]
        ng += 1
    setp()
    print(f'  {ng} groups × 2 histories done; beats with gate argument exactly θ: {tie_grid} (at W0 = 1/2 the baseline input is 1/2 + 1/2 = θ)')
    print('4. Random histories (seed 20261101; 300 histories of length 60; letters cover the four entry patterns and two teaching currents in {0, 1/2, 1, 2};')
    print('    initial weights random rationals in [0, 2]; protocol switches and RELAX ∈ {0, 1/10, 1/2} random)')
    rng = random.Random(20261101)
    tv = [F(0), F(1, 2), F(1), F(2)]
    for _ in range(300):
        rho = rng.choice([F(0), F(1, 10), F(1, 2)])
        Gr = snn_gate(S, rho)
        hist = [(rng.choice(['A', 'B', 'AB', 'none', 'none']), (rng.choice(tv), rng.choice(tv))) for _ in range(60)]
        w0 = [[F(rng.randint(0, 16), 8) for _ in range(S.N_OUT)] for _ in range(S.N_IN)]
        acc(compare_run(S, Gr, hist, w_init=w0, plastic=rng.random() < 0.85, bap=rng.random() < 0.85, relax=rho), 'RAND')
    print('5. Saturation history (tests the clipping branch): A_PLUS = 1/2, A_MINUS = 1/2, strong teaching pairings alternating with presynaptic-only firing')
    setp(A_PLUS=F(1, 2), A_MINUS=F(1, 2))
    Gs = snn_gate(S)
    sat = ([('AB', (F(2), F(2)))] * 6 + [('A', (0, 0))] * 6 + [('B', (F(2), 0)), ('none', (0, 0))] * 6) * 2
    n, c, ti, nb = acc(compare_run(S, Gs, sat), 'SAT')
    setp()
    print(f'  {n} beats, clipping {c}, mismatches {nb}')
    print('6. Totals')
    print(f'  beats compared: {tot["ticks"]}; clipping took effect at {tot["clip"]} places; gate argument exactly at threshold {tot["tie"]} times; mismatches at {len(tot["bad"])} places')
    print('  Conclusion (finite verification): on all histories listed above, the per-beat execution of K_g Definition 2 agrees exactly with snn_core.step, beat by beat and component by component, '
          'including the clipping branch and the at-threshold branch.' if not tot['bad'] else '  Mismatches found: ' + repr(tot['bad'][:10]))

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
~~~

A.3  r\_layer\_witness.py  R-layer event witnesses Ev and slot dependencies (verbatim identical to Appendix A.3 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0)

~~~python
"""R-layer declared instance of 𝒦_g for *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* (main text 3.2, Definitions 4–7): event witness Ev, slot-dependency graph, finite verification of Lemmas R1 (K_t closure), R2 (read set) and R3 (non-interference), and a beat-by-beat check against witnesses generated independently from the frozen SNN log.
Standard library only, exact rational arithmetic. Run: python3 r_layer_witness.py
"""
import sys, random, io, contextlib
from fractions import Fraction as F
sys.dont_write_bytecode = True
import gate_class as GC
~~~
